\tfrac{2}{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{i}}\geqslant(1-\tfrac{2}{\nu_{i}}){\mathbf{1}},\ \ \bar{\beta}_{i}:=\tfrac{\nu_{i}}{2}[{\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot(\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{2}}}-\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{i}})]<1.

Thus, for
\beta_{i}\in(\bar{\beta}_{i},1)
, by Lemma
3.7
and (
3.23
), we have for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{m}\|u{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\nu_{i}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{i}}_{z})}\lesssim\|u{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{z})}+\sum_{i=1}^{m}\|u{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{i}}_{2;{\boldsymbol{a}}})}

\displaystyle\qquad\leqslant C\|F{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}+\varepsilon\sum_{i=1}^{m}\|u{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\nu_{i}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{i}}_{z})}+C_{\varepsilon}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\|f_{i}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{i}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i}}_{z})}.

Choosing
\varepsilon
small enough, we get

\sum_{i=1}^{m}\|u{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\nu_{i}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{i}}_{z})}\lesssim_{C}\|F{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}+\sum_{i=1}^{m}\|f_{i}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{i}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i}}_{z})}.

Substituting it into (
3.23
), we conclude the proof.
∎

4.
Local boundedness of weak solutions

In this section we derive the local bounds of weak solutions for PDE (
3.1
) by considering two cases:
L^{p}
-integrable inhomogeneous
f
and distribution-valued
inhomogeneous
f
.

4.1.
L^{p}
-integrable inhomogeneous
f

We first establish the following local energy estimate.

Lemma 4.1
.

Let
Q\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d}
be a bounded open set and
u\in{\mathscr{V}}_{Q}\cap{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{Q}
a nonnegative weak sub-solution of PDE (
3.1
) in
Q
.
Let
(q_{1},{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}),(\nu_{1},{\boldsymbol{r}}_{1})\in(2,\infty)^{1+2d}
satisfy

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}}+\tfrac{2}{q_{1}}<1,\ \tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}}+\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{1}}=\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{2}}},\ \tfrac{1}{q_{1}}+\tfrac{1}{\nu_{1}}=\tfrac{1}{2}.

(4.1)

Under (
3.2
),
there is a constant
C=C(Q,\kappa_{0},\kappa_{1},\nu_{1},{\boldsymbol{r}}_{1})>0
such that for any nonnegagive
\eta\in C^{\infty}_{c}(Q)
and
t\in{\mathbb{R}}
,

\|u\eta{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathscr{V}}}\lesssim_{C}\Big(\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q}b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{1}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}}_{z})}+\Xi_{\eta}\Big)\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q}u{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\nu_{1}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{1}}_{z})}+\|\langle\!\!\!\langle f\eta,u\eta\rangle\!\!\!\rangle{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{1}_{t}}^{1/2},

where
{\mathcal{I}}_{t}={\mathbf{1}}_{(-\infty,t]}
and

\displaystyle\Xi_{\eta}:=\|\partial_{t}\eta\|^{1/2}_{\infty}+\|v\cdot\nabla_{x}\eta\|^{1/2}_{\infty}+\|\nabla_{v}\eta\|_{\infty}.

(4.2)

Proof.

By (
3.6
), we have for any
t\in{\mathbb{R}}
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}|(u\eta)(t)|^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d}}u^{2}\eta(\partial_{t}\eta-v\cdot\nabla_{x}\eta){\mathcal{I}}_{t}-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d}}\langle a\cdot\nabla_{v}u,\nabla_{v}(u\eta^{2})\rangle{\mathcal{I}}_{t}

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d}}(b\cdot\nabla_{v}u)u\eta^{2}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d}}f\,u\eta^{2}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}.

Noting that by Young’s inequality,

\displaystyle\langle a\cdot\nabla_{v}u,\nabla_{v}(u\eta^{2})\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle a\cdot\nabla_{v}u,\nabla_{v}u\rangle\eta^{2}+2\langle a\cdot\nabla_{v}u,\eta\nabla_{v}\eta\rangle u

\displaystyle\geqslant\kappa_{0}|\eta\nabla_{v}u|^{2}-\kappa_{1}|\eta\nabla u|\,|u\nabla_{v}\eta|\geqslant\tfrac{\kappa_{0}}{2}|\eta\nabla_{v}u|^{2}-\tfrac{\kappa_{1}}{2\kappa_{0}}|u\nabla_{v}\eta|^{2},

we have

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathcal{I}}^{t}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\langle a\cdot\nabla_{v}u,\nabla_{v}(u\eta^{2})\rangle

\displaystyle\geqslant\tfrac{\kappa_{0}}{2}\|\eta\nabla_{v}u{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}-\tfrac{\kappa_{1}}{2\kappa_{0}}\|u\nabla_{v}\eta{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}.

By (
4.1
), Hölder’s inequality and Young’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d}}(b\cdot\nabla_{v}u)u\eta^{2}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\eta\nabla_{v}u{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q}b{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{1}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}}_{z})}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q}u{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\nu_{1}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{1}}_{z})}

\displaystyle\leqslant\tfrac{\kappa_{0}}{4}\|\eta\nabla_{v}u{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}+\tfrac{1}{\kappa_{0}}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q}b{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{1}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}}_{z})}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q}u{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\nu_{1}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{1}}_{z})}.

Combining the above estimates, we obtain that for any
t\in{\mathbb{R}}
,

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\|(u\eta)(t)\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{z}}+\tfrac{\kappa_{0}}{4}\|\nabla_{v}u\eta{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\Big(\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{\infty}+\|v\cdot\nabla_{x}\eta\|_{\infty}+\tfrac{\kappa_{1}}{2\kappa_{0}}\|\nabla_{v}\eta\|^{2}_{\infty}\Big)\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q}u{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}

\displaystyle+\tfrac{1}{\kappa_{0}}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q}b{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{1}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}}_{z})}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q}u{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\nu_{1}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{1}}_{z})}+\|\langle\!\!\!\langle f\eta,u\eta\rangle\!\!\!\rangle{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{1}_{t}},

which in turn implies the desired estimate by noting that for some
C=C(Q,\nu_{1},{\boldsymbol{r}}_{1})>0
,

\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q}u{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}\leqslant C\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q}u{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\nu_{1}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{1}}_{z})}.

The proof is complete.
∎

Recalling
Q_{r}
being defined by (
3.4
), we make the following assumption about the drift
b
:

(H
b
)

Suppose that
\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{2}}b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{1}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}}_{z})}\leqslant\kappa_{2}
for some
(q_{1},{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1})\in(2,\infty)^{1+2d}
with

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}}+\tfrac{2}{q_{1}}<1,\ \ \tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}}<(\tfrac{1}{2}-\tfrac{1}{q_{1}}){\mathbf{1}}.

(4.3)

Remark 4.2
.

The second condition comes from the first condition in (
3.16
).
Note that if
{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}=(p_{1},\cdots,p_{1})\in(2,\infty)^{2d}
, then

{\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}}+\tfrac{2}{q_{1}}<1\Rightarrow\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}}<(\tfrac{1}{2}-\tfrac{1}{q_{1}}){\mathbf{1}}.

Now we can show the following main result of this subsection.

Theorem 4.3
.

Under (
3.2
) and
(H
b
)
, for any
(q_{0},{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0})\in(1,\infty)^{1+2d}
with

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}}+\tfrac{2}{q_{0}}<2,\ \ \tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}}<(1-\tfrac{1}{q_{0}}){\mathbf{1}},

(4.4)

there is a constant
C=C(\kappa_{i},q_{i},{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i})>0
such that for any weak sub-solution
u\in{\mathscr{V}}_{Q_{2}}\cap{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{Q_{2}}
,

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{1}}u^{+}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}}+\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{1}}\nabla_{v}u^{+}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}\lesssim_{C}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{2}}u^{+}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}+\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{2}}f{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{0}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}}_{z})},\ \ \forall|t|\leqslant 1.

(4.5)

Proof.

Let
u\in{\mathscr{V}}_{Q_{2}}\cap{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{Q_{2}}
be a weak sub-solution and for fixed
t\in[-1,1]
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{Q}^{t}_{\tau}:=Q_{\tau}\cap((-\infty,t)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}).

(4.6)

To show the local upper bound estimate in (
4.5
), by Theorem
2.10
, it suffices to show that

u\in{\mathcal{D}}{\mathcal{G}}^{+}_{{\mathscr{I}}_{0}}(\widetilde{Q}^{t}_{\cdot})\mbox{ with ${\mathcal{A}}=\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{2}}f{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{0}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}}_{z})}$,}

where
{\mathscr{I}}_{0}
is an open index subset defined by (see (
3.16
))

{\mathscr{I}}_{0}:=\Big\{(\nu,{\boldsymbol{r}})\in(2,\infty)^{1+2d}:\tfrac{2}{{\boldsymbol{r}}}>(1-\tfrac{2}{\nu}){\mathbf{1}},\ {\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot(\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{r}}}-\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{2}}})+\tfrac{2}{\nu}>0\Big\}.

More precisely, we want to show that for any

(\nu,{\boldsymbol{r}})\in{\mathscr{I}}_{0}
, there is a constant
C>0
only depending on
\kappa_{0},\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2}
,
\nu,{\boldsymbol{r}}
and
q_{i},{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i},i=0,1
such that
for any
1\leqslant\tau<\sigma\leqslant 2
and
|t|\leqslant 1
,
\kappa\geqslant 0
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&(\sigma-\tau)^{2}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{\tau}}(u-\kappa)^{+}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\nu}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{z})}\lesssim_{C}\sum_{i=0,1}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{\sigma}}(u-\kappa)^{+}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\nu_{i}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{i}}_{z})}\\
&\qquad\qquad\quad+\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{2}}f{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{0}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}}_{z})}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{\{u>\kappa\}\cap Q_{\sigma}}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\nu_{0}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{0}}_{z})},\end{split}

(4.7)

where for
i=0,1
,
(\nu_{i},{\boldsymbol{r}}_{i})\in{\mathscr{I}}_{0}
is determined by
(q_{i},{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i})
.

We follow the idea of
[
16
]
and divide the proof into three steps.

(
Step 1
) Let
1\leqslant\tau<\sigma\leqslant 2
and
\bar{\tau}:=\frac{\tau+\sigma}{2}
. Let

\eta_{0}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(Q_{\bar{\tau}};[0,1])

and
\eta_{1}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(Q_{\sigma};[0,1])
with
\eta_{0}|_{Q_{\tau}}\equiv 1
and
\eta_{1}|_{Q_{\bar{\tau}}}\equiv 1
and satisfy that
for some universal constant
C>0
,

\displaystyle\Xi_{\eta_{i}}=\|\partial_{t}\eta_{i}\|^{1/2}_{\infty}+\|v\cdot\nabla_{x}\eta_{i}\|^{1/2}_{\infty}+\|\nabla_{v}\eta_{i}\|_{\infty}\leqslant C(\sigma-\tau)^{-1},\ i=0,1.

(4.8)

Since by Lemma
3.5
,
(u-\kappa)^{+}
is a nonnegative weak sub-solution of PDE (
3.1
) in
Q_{2}
, without loss of generality, we may assume
u
itself being
a nonnegative weak sub-solution of PDE (
3.1
), and prove (
4.7
) for
u
in place of
(u-\kappa)^{+}
.
Note that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(u\eta_{0})

\displaystyle\leqslant\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}(a\cdot\nabla_{v}(u\eta_{0}))+v\cdot\nabla_{x}(u\eta_{0})-\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}(a\cdot\nabla_{v}\eta_{0}u)

\displaystyle-\langle a\cdot\nabla_{v}u,\nabla_{v}\eta_{0}\rangle+(b\cdot\nabla_{v}u)\eta_{0}+(\partial_{t}\eta_{0}-v\cdot\nabla_{x}\eta_{0})u+f{\mathbf{1}}_{\{u>0\}}\eta_{0}

\displaystyle=\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}(a\cdot\nabla_{v}(u\eta_{0}))+v\cdot\nabla_{x}(u\eta_{0})+\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}F+f_{1}+f_{2}+f_{3},

(4.9)

where

F:=-(a\cdot\nabla_{v}\eta_{0})u,\ f_{1}:=(\partial_{t}\eta_{0}-v\cdot\nabla_{x}\eta_{0})u,

and

f_{2}:=f{\mathbf{1}}_{\{u>0\}}\eta_{0},\ \ f_{3}:=(b\eta_{0}-a\cdot\nabla_{v}\eta_{0})\cdot\nabla_{v}u.

Fix
t\in[-1,1]
. By supp(
\eta_{0}
)
\subset Q_{\sigma}
, it is easy to see that

\displaystyle\|F{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\|(a\cdot\nabla_{v}\eta_{0})u{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}\leqslant\kappa_{1}\Xi_{\eta_{0}}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{\sigma}}u{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}},

(4.10)

and

\displaystyle\|f_{1}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\|(\partial_{t}\eta_{0}-v\cdot\nabla_{x}\eta_{0})u{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}\lesssim\Xi_{\eta_{0}}^{2}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{\sigma}}u{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}.

(4.11)

For
f_{2}
, let
(\nu_{0},{\boldsymbol{r}}_{0})\in(2,\infty)^{1+2d}
and
(\bar{q}_{0},\bar{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0})\in(1,2)^{1+2d}
be defined by

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{q_{0}}+\tfrac{2}{\nu_{0}}=1,\ \tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}}+\tfrac{2}{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{0}}={\mathbf{1}},\ \tfrac{1}{\bar{q}_{0}}=\tfrac{1}{q_{0}}+\tfrac{1}{\nu_{0}},\ \ \tfrac{1}{\bar{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}}=\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}}+\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{0}}.

(4.12)

By Hölder’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\|f_{2}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\bar{q}_{0}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{\bar{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}}_{z})}=\|f{\mathbf{1}}_{\{u>0\}}\eta_{0}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\bar{q}_{0}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{\bar{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}}_{z})}\leqslant\|f\eta_{0}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{0}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}}_{z})}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{\{u{\mathcal{I}}_{t}>0\}\cap Q_{\sigma}}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\nu_{0}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{0}}_{z})}.

(4.13)

For
f_{3}
, let
(\bar{q}_{1},\bar{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1})\in(1,2)^{1+2d}
be defined by

\tfrac{1}{q_{1}}+\tfrac{1}{2}=\tfrac{1}{\bar{q}_{1}},\ \ \tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}}+\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{2}}}=\tfrac{1}{\bar{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}}.

Since
\eta_{1}=1
on the support of
\eta_{0}
, by Hölder’s inequality and Lemma
4.1
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|f_{3}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\bar{q}_{1}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{\bar{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}}_{z})}&=\|(b\eta_{0}-a\cdot\nabla_{v}\eta_{0})\cdot\nabla_{v}u\eta_{1}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\bar{q}_{1}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{\bar{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}}_{z})}\\
&\leqslant\|b\eta_{0}-a\cdot\nabla_{v}\eta_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{1}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}}_{z})}\|\nabla_{v}u\eta_{1}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}\\
&\lesssim\big(\kappa_{2}+\kappa_{1}\Xi_{\eta_{0}}\big)\Big((\kappa_{2}+\Xi_{\eta_{1}})\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{\sigma}}u{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\nu_{1}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{1}}_{z})}+\|fu\eta_{1}^{2}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{1}}^{1/2}\Big),\end{split}

(4.14)

where
(\nu_{1},{\boldsymbol{r}}_{1})\in(2,\infty)^{1+2d}
is defined by (
4.1
).
Moreover, by (
4.12
) and Hölder’s inequality,

\displaystyle\|fu\eta_{1}^{2}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{1}}\lesssim\|f\eta_{1}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{0}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}}_{z})}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{\{u{\mathcal{I}}_{t}>0\}\cap Q_{\sigma}}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\nu_{0}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{0}}_{z})}\|u\eta_{1}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\nu_{0}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{0}}_{z})}.

Substituting this into (
4.14
) and by Young’s inequality, we get

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|f_{3}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\bar{q}_{1}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{\bar{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}}_{z})}&\lesssim\big(\kappa_{2}+\kappa_{1}\Xi_{\eta_{0}}\big)\Big((\kappa_{2}+\Xi_{\eta_{1}})\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{\sigma}}u{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\nu_{1}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{1}}_{z})}\\
&+\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{\sigma}}u{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\nu_{0}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{0}}_{z})}+\|f\eta_{1}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{0}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}}_{z})}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{\{u{\mathcal{I}}_{t}>0\}\cap Q_{\sigma}}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\nu_{0}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{0}}_{z})}\Big).\end{split}

(4.15)

Note that by (
4.4
) and (
4.3
), for
i=0,1
,
(\nu_{i},{\boldsymbol{r}}_{i})\in{\mathscr{I}}_{0}
, i.e.,

\tfrac{2}{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{i}}>(1-\tfrac{2}{\nu_{i}}){\mathbf{1}},\ {\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot(\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{i}}-\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{2}}})+\tfrac{2}{\nu_{i}}>0.

(
Step 2
) For
\beta\in(0,1)
and
|t|\leqslant 1
, we define

{\mathscr{A}}^{\beta}_{t}:=\|w{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathscr{V}}}+\|w{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{2;{\boldsymbol{a}}})}.

Let
(\bar{q}_{i},\bar{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i})\in(1,2)^{1+2d}
,
i=0,1
be defined as above. By (
4.4
) and (
4.3
), one sees that

\tfrac{1}{\bar{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i}}<(\tfrac{3}{2}-\tfrac{1}{\bar{q}_{i}}){\mathbf{1}},\ \ \ {\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot(\tfrac{1}{\bar{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i}}-\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{2}}})+\tfrac{2}{\bar{q}_{i}}<2,\ i=0,1.

Thus, by Theorem
3.9
, there is a unique weak solution
w
to the following PDE:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}w=\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}(a\cdot\nabla_{v}w)+v\cdot\nabla_{x}w+\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}F+f_{1}+f_{2}+f_{3},\ \ w|_{t\leqslant-\bar{\tau}^{2}}=0

(4.16)

so that for any
\beta\in(0,1)
and
|t|\leqslant 1
,

\displaystyle{\mathscr{A}}^{\beta}_{t}\lesssim\|F{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}+\|f_{1}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}+\|f_{2}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\bar{q}_{0}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{\bar{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}}_{z})}+\|f_{3}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\bar{q}_{1}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{\bar{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}}_{z})}.

Furthermore, by (
4.10
), (
4.11
), (
4.13
) and (
4.15
), we get

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathscr{A}}^{\beta}_{t}&\lesssim(1+\Xi^{2}_{\eta_{0}})\|u\eta_{1}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}+\|f\eta_{0}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{0}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}}_{z})}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{\{u{\mathcal{I}}_{t}>0\}\cap Q_{\sigma}}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\nu_{0}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{0}}_{z})}\\
&\quad+\big(1+\Xi_{\eta_{0}}\big)\Big((1+\Xi_{\eta_{1}})\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{\sigma}}u{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\nu_{1}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{1}}_{z})}+\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{\sigma}}u{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\nu_{0}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{0}}_{z})}\Big)\\
&\quad+\big(1+\Xi_{\eta_{0}}\big)\|f\eta_{1}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{0}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}}_{z})}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{\{u{\mathcal{I}}_{t}>0\}\cap Q_{\sigma}}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\nu_{0}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{0}}_{z})}.\end{split}

(4.17)

Using (
4.8
) and
\|u\eta_{1}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}\lesssim\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{\sigma}}u{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\nu_{1}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{1}}_{z})}
,
we find that for any
\beta\in(0,1)
and
1\leqslant\tau<\sigma\leqslant 2
,

\displaystyle(\sigma-\tau)^{2}{\mathscr{A}}^{\beta}_{t}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{i=0,1}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{\sigma}}u{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\nu_{i}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{i}}_{z})}+\|f\eta_{1}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{0}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}}_{z})}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{\{u{\mathcal{I}}_{t}>0\}\cap Q_{\sigma}}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\nu_{0}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{0}}_{z})},

(4.18)

where the implicit constant is independent of
\tau,\sigma
and
t
, but may depend on
\beta
.

(
Step 3
) By (
4.9
) and (
4.16
), one sees that
\bar{w}:=u\eta_{0}-w\in{\mathscr{V}}
is a weak sub-solution of

\partial_{t}\bar{w}\leqslant\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}(a\cdot\nabla_{v}w)+v\cdot\nabla_{x}\bar{w},\ \ \bar{w}|_{t\leqslant-\bar{\tau}^{2}}=0.

By Lemma
3.5
,
\bar{w}^{+}
is still a weak sub-solution of the above equation.
By Lemma
4.1
with
b=f=0
,
\eta=\chi_{R}
, where
\chi_{R}
is the same as in (
3.21
),
and letting
R\to\infty
, it is easy to see that

\bar{w}^{+}\equiv 0\Rightarrow 0\leqslant u\eta_{0}\leqslant w.

For given
(\nu,{\boldsymbol{r}})\in{\mathscr{I}}_{0}
, let

\bar{\beta}:=\tfrac{\nu}{2}[{\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot(\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{2}}}-\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{r}}})]\in(0,1).

By Lemma
3.7
with
q=2
and
\beta\in(\bar{\beta},1)
, we have

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{\tau}}u{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\nu}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{z})}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|u\eta_{0}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\nu}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{z})}\lesssim\|w{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\nu}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{z})}\lesssim{\mathscr{A}}^{\beta}_{t},

which combining with (
4.18
) yields (
4.7
). Thus, by Theorem
2.10
,

\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{3/2}}u^{+}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}}\lesssim_{C}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{2}}u^{+}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}+\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{2}}f{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{0}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}}_{z})}.

Finally, let
\chi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(Q_{3/2})
be nonnegative and
\chi=1
on
Q_{1}
. By (
4.5
) with
\eta=\chi
and the above estimate, we also have

\displaystyle\|u^{+}\chi{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathscr{V}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{C}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{3/2}}u^{+}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\nu_{1}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{1}}_{z})}+\|\langle\!\!\!\langle f\chi,u^{+}\chi\rangle\!\!\!\rangle{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{1}_{t}}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{C}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{3/2}}u^{+}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}}+\|f\chi{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{1}}^{1/2}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{3/2}}u^{+}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{C}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{2}}u^{+}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}+\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{2}}f{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{0}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}}_{z})},

where we have used that
\|f\chi{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{1}}\lesssim\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{2}}f{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{0}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}}_{z})}
.
The proof is complete.
∎

4.2.
Distribution-valued inhomogeneous
f

In this subsection, we consider the case of
f
being a distribution and suppose that

(H
{}^{\prime}_{f}
)

f=g+\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}G
, where
g\in{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{0}}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{-\alpha_{0}}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0};{\boldsymbol{a}}})\cap{\mathbb{L}}^{1}

for some
\alpha_{0}\in[0,1)
and
(q_{0},{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0})\in(1,\infty)^{1+2d}
with

\displaystyle 1-\alpha_{0}<\tfrac{2}{q_{0}},\ \tfrac{2}{q_{0}}+{\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}}<2-2\alpha_{0},

(4.19)

and
G\in{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{2}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{2}}_{z})
for some
q_{2}\in(2,4)
and
{\boldsymbol{p}}_{2}\in(2,\infty)^{2d}
with

\displaystyle\tfrac{2}{q_{2}}+{\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{2}}<1.

(4.20)

(H
{}^{\prime}_{b}
)

\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{2}}b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{1}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}}_{z})}\leqslant\kappa_{2}
for some
q_{1}\in(2,4)
and
{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}\in(2,\infty)^{2d}
with

\displaystyle\tfrac{2}{q_{1}}+{\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}}<1.

(4.21)

Remark 4.4
.

Condition
(H
{}^{\prime}_{b}
)
does not imply
(H
b
)
due to the extra requirement
\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}}+\frac{{\mathbf{1}}}{q_{1}}<\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{2}}}
in (
4.3
).
In other words, when
q_{1}\in(2,4)
, we can drop the assumption
\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}}+\frac{{\mathbf{1}}}{q_{1}}<\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{2}}}
in
(H
b
)
in the following theorem
because we shall use Lemma
3.7
with
q=4
and
\beta\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
(see (
3.17
)).

Theorem 4.5
.

Under (
3.2
),
(H
{}^{\prime}_{b}
)
and
(H
{}^{\prime}_{f}
)
,
there is a constant
C>0
only depending on
\alpha_{0},\kappa_{i},q_{i},{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i}
such that for any weak solution
u\in{\mathscr{V}}_{Q_{2}}\cap{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{Q_{2}}
of PDE (
3.1
)
and
|t|\leqslant 1
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{1}}u{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}}+\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{1}}\nabla_{v}u{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}\lesssim_{C}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{2}}u{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}+\|g\chi_{2}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{0}}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{-\alpha_{0}}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}+\|G\chi_{2}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{2}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{2}}_{z})},\end{split}

(4.22)

and for any
\beta\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\|u\chi_{1}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{4}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta/2}_{\boldsymbol{2};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}\lesssim_{C}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{2}}u{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}+\|g\chi_{2}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{0}}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{-\alpha_{0}}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}+\|G\chi_{2}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{2}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{2}}_{z})},

(4.23)

where
\chi_{1}
and
\chi_{2}
are defined by (
3.21
).

Proof.

Let
u\in{\mathscr{V}}_{Q_{2}}\cap{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{Q_{2}}
be a weak solution of PDE (
3.1
).
For proving (
4.22
), by Theorem
2.10
, it suffices to show that for fixed
t\in[-1,1]
,

u\in{\mathcal{D}}{\mathcal{G}}^{+}_{{\mathscr{I}}_{1}}(\widetilde{Q}^{t}_{\cdot})\mbox{ with ${\mathcal{A}}=\|g\chi_{2}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{0}}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{-\alpha_{0}}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}+\|G\chi_{2}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{2}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{2}}_{z})}$,}

where
\widetilde{Q}^{t}
is defined by (
4.6
) and
{\mathscr{I}}_{1}
is an open index subset defined by

\displaystyle{\mathscr{I}}_{1}:=\Big\{(\nu,{\boldsymbol{r}})\in(4,\infty)\times(2,\infty)^{2d}:{\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot(\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{r}}}-\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{2}}})+\tfrac{2}{\nu}>0\Big\}.

(4.24)

More precisely, we want to show that for any

(\nu,{\boldsymbol{r}})\in{\mathscr{I}}_{1}
, there is a constant
C>0
only depending on
\nu,{\boldsymbol{r}}
and
\alpha_{0}
,
\kappa_{i}
,
q_{i},{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i},i=0,1,2
such that
for any
1\leqslant\tau<\sigma\leqslant 2
and
|t|\leqslant 1
,
\kappa\geqslant 0
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&(\sigma-\tau)\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{\tau}}w{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\nu}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{z})}\lesssim_{C}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{\sigma}}w{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\nu_{1}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{1}}_{z})}\\
&\quad+{\mathcal{A}}\Big(\|{\mathbf{1}}_{\{w>0\}\cap Q_{\sigma}}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\nu_{0}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{0}}_{z})}+\|{\mathbf{1}}_{\{w>0\}\cap Q_{\sigma}}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\nu_{2}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{2}}_{z})}\Big),\end{split}

(4.25)

where
(\nu_{i},{\boldsymbol{r}}_{i})\in{\mathscr{I}}_{1}
are determined by
(q_{i},{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i})
,
i=0,1,2
and

w:=(u-\kappa)^{+}.

We divide the proof into four steps.

(
Step 1
) Let
1\leqslant\tau<\sigma\leqslant 2
and
\eta\in C^{\infty}_{c}(Q_{\sigma};[0,1])
with
\eta|_{Q_{\tau}}\equiv 1
and satisfy that
for some universal constant
C>0
,

\displaystyle\Xi_{\eta}:=1+\|\partial_{t}\eta\|^{1/2}_{\infty}+\|v\cdot\nabla_{x}\eta\|^{1/2}_{\infty}+\|\nabla_{v}\eta\|_{\infty}\leqslant C(\sigma-\tau)^{-1}.

(4.26)

Note that by (ii) of Lemma
3.5
,
w^{2}
is a weak solution of PDE

\partial_{t}w^{2}=\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}(a\cdot\nabla_{v}w^{2})+v\cdot\nabla_{x}w^{2}+b\cdot\nabla_{v}w^{2}-2\mathrm{tr}(a\cdot\nabla_{v}w\otimes\nabla_{v}w)+2fw.

Let

\bar{w}:=w\eta.

By definition, one sees that
\bar{w}^{2}
is a nonnegative weak solution of the following PDE

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\bar{w}^{2}=\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}(a\cdot\nabla_{v}\bar{w}^{2})+v\cdot\nabla_{x}\bar{w}^{2}+h,

(4.27)

where, thanks to
f=g+\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}G
,
h
is given by

\displaystyle h

\displaystyle:=-\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}(a\cdot\nabla_{v}\eta^{2}w^{2})-\mathrm{tr}(a\cdot\nabla_{v}w^{2}\otimes\nabla_{v}\eta^{2})-2\mathrm{tr}(a\cdot\nabla_{v}w\otimes\nabla_{v}w)\eta^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+(\partial_{t}\eta^{2}-v\cdot\nabla_{x}\eta^{2})w^{2}+(b\cdot\nabla_{v}w^{2})\eta^{2}+2(g+\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}G)w\eta^{2}.

Integrating both sides of (
4.27
) over
{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}
, by the integration by parts, we obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\int\bar{w}^{2}=\int h=\int f_{1}+\int f_{2}+\int f_{3}+2\langle\!\!\!\langle g\eta,\bar{w}\rangle\!\!\!\rangle,

(4.28)

where
\langle\!\!\!\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle\!\!\!\rangle
stands for the duality between
{\mathbb{L}}^{1}_{z}
and
{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{z}
, and for
\bar{b}:=b\eta-2a\cdot\nabla\eta
,

\displaystyle f_{1}

\displaystyle:=-2\mathrm{tr}(a\cdot\nabla_{v}w\otimes\nabla_{v}w)\eta^{2},

\displaystyle f_{2}

\displaystyle:=(\partial_{t}\eta^{2}-v\cdot\nabla_{x}\eta^{2})w^{2}+(\bar{b}\cdot\nabla_{v}w^{2})\eta,

\displaystyle f_{3}

\displaystyle:=-2G\cdot(\nabla_{v}w\eta^{2}+\nabla\eta^{2}w).

For
f_{1}
, by the uniform ellipticity of
a
, we have

\displaystyle\int f_{1}

\displaystyle=-\int\mathrm{tr}(a\cdot\nabla_{v}w\otimes\nabla_{v}w)\eta^{2}\leqslant-\kappa_{0}\int|\nabla_{v}w\eta|^{2}=-\kappa_{0}\|\nabla_{v}w\eta\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{z}}^{2}.

For
f_{2}
, since
\eta(t,z)\in[0,1]
has support in
Q_{\sigma}
, by Hölder and Young’s inequalities, we have

\displaystyle\int|f_{2}|

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\|(|\partial_{t}\eta|+|v\cdot\nabla_{x}\eta|)w^{2}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{1}_{z}}+2\int|(\bar{b}\cdot\nabla_{v}w)w\eta|

\displaystyle\leqslant\Xi^{2}_{\eta}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{\sigma}}w\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{z}}^{2}+\tfrac{\kappa_{0}}{4}\|\nabla_{v}w\eta\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{z}}^{2}+\tfrac{2}{\kappa_{0}}\|\bar{b}w\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{z}}.

For
f_{3}
, by
\nabla_{v}w=\nabla_{v}w{\mathbf{1}}_{w\not=0}
(see
[
15
, Lemma 7.6]
) and Hölder’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int|f_{3}|&\leqslant 2\|G{\mathbf{1}}_{w\not=0}\eta\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{z}}(\|\nabla_{v}w\eta\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{z}}+2\|w\nabla_{v}\eta\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{z}})\\
&\leqslant\tfrac{\kappa_{0}}{4}\|\nabla_{v}w\eta\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{z}}+\tfrac{\kappa_{0}}{2}\|w\nabla_{v}\eta\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{z}}^{2}+\tfrac{4}{\kappa_{0}}\|G{\mathbf{1}}_{w\not=0}\eta\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{z}}^{2}.\end{split}

(4.29)

Integrating both sides of (
4.28
) from
-4
to
t
and combining the above calculations, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\bar{w}(t)\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{z}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\int^{t}_{-4}\Big(-\tfrac{\kappa_{0}}{2}\|\nabla_{v}w\eta\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{z}}+C\Xi^{2}_{\eta}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{\sigma}}w\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{z}}^{2}+\tfrac{2}{\kappa_{0}}\|\bar{b}w\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{z}}+\tfrac{4}{\kappa_{0}}\|G{\mathbf{1}}_{w\not=0}\eta\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{z}}^{2}+2|\langle\!\!\!\langle g\eta,\bar{w}\rangle\!\!\!\rangle|\Big){\mathord{{\rm d}}}s,

which yields by taking supremum in
t
that for fixed
t\in[-1,1]
,

\displaystyle\|\bar{w}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{z})}+\|\nabla_{v}w{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\eta\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}\lesssim\Xi^{2}_{\eta}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{\sigma}}w{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}+\|\bar{b}w{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}+\|G{\mathbf{1}}_{w\not=0}\eta\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}^{2}+\|\langle\!\!\!\langle g\eta,\bar{w}\rangle\!\!\!\rangle{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{1}_{t}}.

(4.30)

(
Step 2
) Note that in the distributional sense,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\bar{w}^{2}

\displaystyle=\Delta_{v}\bar{w}^{2}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}\bar{w}^{2}+\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}F+f_{1}+f_{2}+f_{3}+2gw\eta^{2},

where
f_{1},f_{2},f_{3}
are defined as above and

F:=(a-{\mathbb{I}})\cdot\nabla_{v}\bar{w}^{2}-a\cdot\nabla_{v}\eta^{2}w^{2}+Gw\eta^{2}.

By Duhamel’s formula, we have

\displaystyle\bar{w}(t)^{2}

\displaystyle=\int^{t}_{-4}P_{t-s}\big(\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}F+f_{1}+f_{2}+f_{3}+2g\eta\bar{w}\big){\mathord{{\rm d}}}s

\displaystyle=:I_{0}(t)+I_{1}(t)+I_{2}(t)+I_{3}(t)+I_{4}(t).

We estimate each term by Lemma
3.6
as following.

\bullet

For
I_{0}(t)
, by (
3.15
) with
\beta\in(0,1)
and
(\nu,q,\gamma,{\boldsymbol{p}})=(2,2,-1,\boldsymbol{1})
,

\displaystyle\|I_{0}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}((a-{\mathbb{I}})\cdot\nabla_{v}\bar{w}^{2}-a\cdot\nabla_{v}\eta^{2}w^{2}+Gw\eta^{2}){\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{-1}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|((a-{\mathbb{I}})\cdot\nabla_{v}\bar{w}^{2}-a\cdot\nabla_{v}\eta^{2}w^{2}+Gw\eta^{2}){\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{1}_{z})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\bar{w}\nabla_{v}\bar{w}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{1}_{z})}+\|w\nabla_{v}\eta\bar{w}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{1}_{z})}+\|G\bar{w}\eta{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{1}_{z})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(\|\nabla_{v}\bar{w}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}+\|w\nabla_{v}\eta{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}+\|G{\mathbf{1}}_{w\not=0}\eta\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}\big)\|\bar{w}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{z})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla_{v}w\eta{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}+\|w\nabla_{v}\eta{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}+\|G{\mathbf{1}}_{w\not=0}\eta\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}^{2}+\|\bar{w}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{z})}^{2}.

\bullet

For
I_{1}(t)
, by (
3.15
) with
\beta\in(0,1)
and
(\nu,q,\gamma,{\boldsymbol{p}})=(2,1,0,\boldsymbol{1})
,

\displaystyle\|I_{1}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathrm{tr}(a\cdot\nabla_{v}w\otimes\nabla_{v}w)\eta^{2}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{1}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{0}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathrm{tr}(a\cdot\nabla_{v}w\otimes\nabla_{v}w)\eta^{2}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{1}}\lesssim\|\nabla_{v}w\eta{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}^{2}.

\bullet

For
I_{2}(t)
, by (
3.15
) with
\beta\in(0,1)
and
(\nu,q,\gamma,{\boldsymbol{p}})=(2,1,0,\boldsymbol{1})
,

\displaystyle\|I_{2}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|((\partial_{t}\eta^{2}-v\cdot\nabla_{x}\eta^{2})w^{2}-(\bar{b}\cdot\nabla_{v}w^{2})\eta){\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Xi^{2}_{\eta}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{\sigma}}w{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}+\|\bar{b}w{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}\|\nabla_{v}w\eta{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}.

\bullet

For
I_{3}(t)
, by (
3.15
) with
\beta\in(0,1)
and
(\nu,q,\gamma,{\boldsymbol{p}})=(2,1,0,1)
, as in (
4.29
),

\displaystyle\|I_{3}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|G\cdot(\nabla_{v}w\eta^{2}+\nabla\eta^{2}w){\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{1}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|G{\mathbf{1}}_{w\not=0}\eta\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}(\|\nabla_{v}w\eta\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}+2\|w\nabla_{v}\eta\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}).

\bullet

For
I_{4}(t)
, by (
3.15
) with
\beta\in(0,1)
and
(\nu,q,\gamma,{\boldsymbol{p}})=(2,\frac{2}{2-\alpha_{0}},-\alpha_{0},{\mathbf{1}})
,

\displaystyle\|I_{4}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}\lesssim\|g\eta\bar{w}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{-\alpha_{0}}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}.

Combining the above calculations, we obtain that for
q=\frac{2}{2-\alpha_{0}}
and any
\beta\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\bar{w}^{2}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}&\lesssim\|\bar{w}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{z})}+\|\nabla_{v}w\eta{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}+\|G{\mathbf{1}}_{w\not=0}\eta{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}^{2}\\
&+\Xi^{2}_{\eta}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{\sigma}}w{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}+\|\bar{b}w{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}\|\nabla_{v}w\eta{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}+\|g\eta\bar{w}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{-\alpha_{0}}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}.\end{split}

(4.31)

Note that by Lemma
2.6
,

\displaystyle\|\bar{w}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{4}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta/2}_{\boldsymbol{2};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}\lesssim\|\bar{w}^{2}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}.

(4.32)

If we define

{\mathscr{A}}^{\beta}_{t}:=\|\bar{w}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{4}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta/2}_{\boldsymbol{2};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}+\|\bar{w}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{z})}+\|\nabla_{v}w\eta{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}},

then by (
4.32
), (
4.31
) and (
4.30
), we get for
q=\frac{2}{2-\alpha_{0}}
and any
\beta\in(0,1)
,
|t|<1
,

\displaystyle{\mathscr{A}}^{\beta}_{t}\lesssim\Xi^{2}_{\eta}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{\sigma}}w{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}+\|\bar{b}w{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}+\|G{\mathbf{1}}_{w\not=0}\eta{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}^{2}+\|\langle\!\!\!\langle g\eta,\bar{w}\rangle\!\!\!\rangle{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{1}_{t}}+\|g\eta\bar{w}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{-\alpha_{0}}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}.

(4.33)

(
Step 3
) In this step we estimate the last two terms by Lemma
2.5
and Hölder’s inequality.
Let
q_{2}\in(0,\infty)
and
{\boldsymbol{p}}_{2}\in(1,2]^{2d}
be defined by

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{q_{0}}+\tfrac{1}{q_{2}}=\tfrac{1}{q}=1-\tfrac{\alpha_{0}}{2},\ \ \tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}}+\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{2}}=1.

(4.34)

By Lemma
2.5
and Hölder’s inequality, we have for any
\alpha_{2}>\alpha_{0}
,

\displaystyle\|\langle\!\!\!\langle g\eta,\bar{w}\rangle\!\!\!\rangle{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{1}_{t}}+\|g\eta\bar{w}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{-\alpha_{0}}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}\lesssim\|g\eta{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{0}}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{-\alpha_{0}}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}\|\bar{w}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{2}}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{\alpha_{2}}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{2};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}.

(4.35)

Now due to (
4.19
), one can choose
\theta\in(2\alpha_{0},1)
being close to
2\alpha_{0}
so that

\displaystyle\tfrac{2}{q_{0}}+{\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}}<2-\alpha_{0}-\tfrac{\theta}{2}.

(4.36)

Let
q_{3}\in(0,\infty)
and
{\boldsymbol{p}}_{3}\in(1,2)^{2d}
be defined by

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{q_{2}}=\tfrac{1-\theta}{q_{3}}+\tfrac{\theta}{4},\ \ \tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{2}}=\tfrac{1-\theta}{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{3}}+\tfrac{\theta}{{\boldsymbol{2}}}.

(4.37)

In particular, since
\frac{\theta}{2}>\alpha_{0}
, one can choose
\beta\in(0,1)
so that

\alpha_{2}:={\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{2}}+(1-\theta)(0-{\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{3}})+\theta(\tfrac{\beta}{2}-\tfrac{|{\boldsymbol{a}}|}{2})=\tfrac{\theta\beta}{2}>\alpha_{0}.

Thus by Gagliado-Nirenberge’s inequality (
2.12
) and Hölder’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\|\bar{w}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{2}}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{\alpha_{2}}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{2};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\bar{w}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{1-\theta}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{3}}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{0}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{3};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}\|\bar{w}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{\theta}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{4}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta/2}_{2;{\boldsymbol{a}}})}\lesssim\|\bar{w}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{1-\theta}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{3}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{3}}_{z})}\|\bar{w}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{\theta}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{4}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta/2}_{2;{\boldsymbol{a}}})}.

On the other hand, for
\nu_{0}:=\frac{(2-\theta)q_{3}}{1-\theta}
and
{\boldsymbol{r}}_{0}:=\frac{(2-\theta){\boldsymbol{p}}_{3}}{1-\theta}
, by Hölder’s inequality,

\displaystyle\|\bar{w}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{1-\theta}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{3}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{3}}_{z})}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|{\mathbf{1}}_{\bar{w}\not=0}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{1-\theta}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\nu_{0}(1-\theta)}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{0}(1-\theta)}_{z})}\|\bar{w}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{1-\theta}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\nu_{0}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{0}}_{z})}

\displaystyle=\|{\mathbf{1}}_{\bar{w}\not=0}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\nu_{0}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{0}}_{z})}\|\bar{w}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{1-\theta}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\nu_{0}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{0}}_{z})}.

Hence,

\displaystyle\|\bar{w}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{2}}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{\alpha_{2}}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{2};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}\lesssim\|{\mathbf{1}}_{\bar{w}\not=0}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\nu_{0}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{0}}_{z})}\|\bar{w}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{1-\theta}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\nu_{0}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{0}}_{z})}\|\bar{w}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{\theta}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{4}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta/2}_{2;{\boldsymbol{a}}})}.

(4.38)

Since
1-\alpha_{0}<\tfrac{2}{q_{0}}
, by (
4.36
), it is easy to see that
(\nu_{0},{\boldsymbol{r}}_{0})\in{\mathscr{I}}_{1}
.

(
Step 4
) Combining (
4.33
), (
4.35
) and (
4.38
) and by Hölder’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle{\mathscr{A}}^{\beta}_{t}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Xi^{2}_{\eta}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{\sigma}}w{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}+\|\bar{b}w{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}+\|G{\mathbf{1}}_{w\not=0}\eta{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}^{2}+\|g\eta{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{0}}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{-\alpha_{0}}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}\|\bar{w}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{2}}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{\alpha_{1}}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{2};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Xi^{2}_{\eta}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{\sigma}}w{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}+\|\bar{b}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{1}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}}_{z})}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{\sigma}}w{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\nu_{1}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{1}}_{z})}+\|G\eta{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{2}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{2}}_{z})}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{\bar{w}\not=0}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\nu_{2}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{2}}_{z})}

\displaystyle\quad+\|g\eta{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{0}}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{-\alpha_{0}}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{\bar{w}\not=0}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\nu_{0}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{0}}_{z})}\|\bar{w}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{1-\theta}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\nu_{0}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{0}}_{z})}\|\bar{w}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{\theta}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{4}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta/2}_{2;{\boldsymbol{a}}})},

where we have used
{\mathbf{1}}_{w\not=0}\eta={\mathbf{1}}_{\bar{w}\not=0}\eta
, and
(\nu_{i},{\boldsymbol{r}}_{i})\in(2,\infty)^{1+2d}
,
i=1,2
, are defined by

\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i}}+\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{i}}=\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{2}}},\ \tfrac{1}{q_{i}}+\tfrac{1}{\nu_{i}}=\tfrac{1}{2}.

Note that by (
4.20
) and (
4.21
),

(\nu_{i},{\boldsymbol{r}}_{i})\in{\mathscr{I}}_{1},\ \ i=1,2.

Recalling
\bar{b}=b\eta-2a\cdot\nabla\eta
,
for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, by Young’s inequality, we arrive at

\displaystyle{\mathscr{A}}^{\beta}_{t}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Xi^{2}_{\eta}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{\sigma}}w{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}+\Xi^{2}_{\eta}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{\sigma}}w{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\nu_{1}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{1}}_{z})}+\|G\eta{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{2}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{2}}_{z})}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{\bar{w}\not=0}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\nu_{2}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{2}}_{z})}

\displaystyle\quad+C_{\varepsilon}\|g\eta{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{0}}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{-\alpha_{0}}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{\bar{w}\not=0}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\nu_{0}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{0}}_{z})}+\varepsilon\Big(\|\bar{w}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\nu_{0}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{0}}_{z})}+\|\bar{w}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{4}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta/2}_{2;{\boldsymbol{a}}})}\Big).

(4.39)

For any
(\nu,{\boldsymbol{r}})\in{\mathscr{I}}_{1}
, one can choose
\beta\in(0,1)
so that
{\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot(\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{r}}}-\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{2}}})+\tfrac{2\beta}{\nu}>0
,
and thus, by Lemma
3.7
with
q=4
and (
3.17
),

\displaystyle\|\bar{w}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\nu}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{z})}\lesssim{\mathscr{A}}^{\beta}_{t},

(4.40)

which, together with
(\nu_{0},{\boldsymbol{r}}_{0})\in{\mathscr{I}}_{1}
, implies that
the last term in (
4.2
) can be absorbed by the left hand side if one lets
\varepsilon
being small enough.
Moreover,

\|G\eta{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{2}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{2}}_{z})}=\|G\chi_{2}\eta{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{2}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{2}}_{z})}\leqslant\|G\chi_{2}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{2}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{2}}_{z})},

and

\|g\eta\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{0}}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{-\alpha_{0}}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0};a})}=\|g\chi_{2}\eta\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{0}}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{-\alpha_{0}}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0};a})}\lesssim\|g\chi_{2}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{0}}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{-\alpha_{0}}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0};a})}\|\eta\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{1}_{\infty;a})}.

Substituting these into (
4.2
) and by (
4.26
), (
4.40
) and

\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{\sigma}}w{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}\lesssim\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{\sigma}}w{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\nu_{1}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{1}}_{z})}
, we obtain (
4.25
) as well as (
4.23
).
∎

Remark 4.6
.

Since
\nu_{0},\nu_{1},\nu_{2}
in (
4.25
) are required to be greater than
4
(see (
4.24
)), we have to require

1-\alpha_{0}<\tfrac{2}{q_{0}}
in (
4.19
) and
q_{1},q_{2}\in(2,4)
in (
4.21
) and (
4.20
).
Note that
q_{i}\in(2,4)
naturally leads to
{\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i}}<\frac{1}{2}
, and when
\alpha_{0}=0
, the condition
1<\tfrac{2}{q_{0}}

leads to
{\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}}<1
.

Remark 4.7
.

If
g=\sum_{i=0}^{m}g_{i}
, where
g_{i}\in{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{i}}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{-\alpha_{i}}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}})
for some
\alpha_{i}\in[0,1)
and
(q_{i},{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i})\in(1,\infty)^{1+2d}
satisfying (
4.19
), then
we still have (
4.22
) with
\|g\chi_{2}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{0}}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{-\alpha_{0}}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}
replaced by

\sum_{i=0}^{m}\|g_{i}\chi_{2}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{i}}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{-\alpha_{i}}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}
.

5.
Global boundness and stability of weak solutions

Fix
r>0
. For
{\boldsymbol{p}}\in[1,\infty]^{2d}
, we introduce the following localized
L^{\boldsymbol{p}}
-space:

\displaystyle|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|f|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}_{z}}:=\sup_{z_{0}}\|f{\mathbf{1}}_{B^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{r}(z_{0})}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}_{z}}=\sup_{z_{0}}\|f(\cdot-z_{0}){\mathbf{1}}_{B^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{r}}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}_{z}},

(5.1)

and for
(q,{\boldsymbol{p}})\in[1,\infty]^{1+2d}
,

\displaystyle|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|f|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{t}(\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}_{z})}:=\sup_{t_{0},z_{0}}\|f(\cdot-t_{0},\cdot-z_{0}){\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{r}}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}_{z})},

(5.2)

and for
\alpha\in{\mathbb{R}}
,

|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|f|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{t}(\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\alpha}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}:=\sup_{t_{0},z_{0}}\|f(\cdot-t_{0},\cdot-z_{0})\chi_{2}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{\alpha}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}})},

where
\chi_{2}
is a cutoff function as in (
3.21
).
By a finitely covering technique, it is easy to see that for different
r,r^{\prime}>0
(cf.
[
39
]
),

\displaystyle\sup_{z_{0}}\|f{\mathbf{1}}_{B^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{r}(z_{0})}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}_{z}}\asymp\sup_{z_{0}}\|f{\mathbf{1}}_{B^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{r^{\prime}}(z_{0})}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}_{z}}

(5.3)

and

\sup_{t_{0},z_{0}}\|f(\cdot-t_{0},\cdot-z_{0}){\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{r}}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}_{z})}\asymp\sup_{t_{0},z_{0}}\|f(\cdot-t_{0},\cdot-z_{0}){\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{r^{\prime}}}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}_{z})}.

In particular, for any
T>0
,

\displaystyle|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|{\mathbf{1}}_{[0,T]}f|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{t}(\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}_{z})}\asymp\sup_{z_{0}}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{[0,T]}f{\mathbf{1}}_{B^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{r}(z_{0})}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}_{z})}\leqslant\left(\int^{T}_{0}|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|f(s)|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}_{z}}^{q}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}s\right)^{1/q},

(5.4)

and for any bounded
Q\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d}
, there is a constant
C=C(Q,d,q,{\boldsymbol{p}})>0
such that

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q}f\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}_{z})}\lesssim_{C}|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|f|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{t}(\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{\boldsymbol{p}}_{z})}.

(5.5)

We also introduce the following localized energy space for later use:

\widetilde{\mathscr{V}}:=\Big\{f\in{\mathbb{L}}^{1}_{loc}:|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|f|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|_{\widetilde{\mathscr{V}}}:=|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|f|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{t}(\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{z})}+|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|\nabla_{v}f|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{t}(\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{z})}<\infty\Big\}.

Using the above notations, we make the following global assumption about drift
b
:

(
\widetilde{\bf H}_{b}
)

Suppose that
|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|b|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{1}}_{t}(\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}}_{z})}\leqslant\kappa_{2}

for some
(q_{1},{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1})\in(2,\infty)^{1+2d}
satisfying (
4.3
).

First of all we derive the following existence and uniqueness result of global weak solutions for FPKE (
3.1
) with initial value
u(t)|_{t\leqslant 0}=0
.

Theorem 5.1
.

Under (
3.2
) and
(
\widetilde{\bf H}_{b}
)
, for any
f\in\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{0}}_{t}(\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}}_{z})

with
(q_{0},{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0})\in(1,\infty)^{1+2d}
satisfying (
4.4
),
and
T>0
, there is a unique global weak solution
u
to PDE (
3.1
) in the sense of Definition
3.1

with initial value
u(t)|_{t\leqslant 0}=0
such that for some
C=C(T,\kappa_{i},q_{i},{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i})>0
and any
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\|u{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}}+|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|u{\mathcal{I}}_{t}|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|_{\widetilde{\mathscr{V}}}\lesssim_{C}|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|f{\mathcal{I}}_{t}|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{0}}_{t}(\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}}_{z})}.

(5.6)

Proof.

We divide the proof into two steps.

(
Step 1
) We first show (
5.6
) for any weak solution
u
.
Without loss of generality, we assume
T=1
and

u(t,x,v)=b(t,x,v)=f(t,x,v)\equiv 0,\ \ \forall t\leqslant 0.

Fix
z_{0}=(x_{0},v_{0})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}
. Let
\Gamma_{t}z_{0}:=(x_{0}+tv_{0},v_{0})
and define

u_{z_{0}}(t,z):=u(t,z-\Gamma_{t}z_{0}),\ a_{z_{0}}(t,z):=a(t,z-\Gamma_{t}z_{0}),\

and

b_{z_{0}}(t,z):=b(t,z-\Gamma_{t}z_{0}),\ f_{z_{0}}(t,z):=f(t,z-\Gamma_{t}z_{0}).

By definition, it is easy to see that
u_{z_{0}}
is a weak solution of PDE (
3.1
)
with coefficients
(a_{z_{0}},b_{z_{0}},f_{z_{0}})
.
By
(
\widetilde{\bf H}^{q_{1}}_{b}
)
and (
5.5
), it is easy to see that

\sup_{z_{0}}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{2}}b_{z_{0}}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{1}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}}_{z})}\leqslant C(\kappa_{2}).

By applying Theorem
4.3
to nonnegative weak sub-solution
u^{+}_{z_{0}}
and
u^{-}_{z_{0}}=(-u_{z_{0}})^{+}
separately,
there is a constant
C=C(\kappa_{i},q_{i},{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i})>0
such that for each
t\in[0,1]
and
z_{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}
,

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{1}}u_{z_{0}}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}}+\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{1}}\nabla_{v}u_{z_{0}}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}\lesssim_{C}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{2}}u_{z_{0}}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}+\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{2}}f_{z_{0}}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{0}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}}_{z})}.

(5.7)

In particular, for each
t\in[0,1]
,

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{1}}u_{z_{0}}(t)\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{z}}

\displaystyle\leqslant{\rm Vol}(Q_{1})\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{1}}u_{z_{0}}(t)\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{z}}^{2}\lesssim\int^{t}_{0}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{2}}u_{z_{0}}(s)\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{z}}^{2}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}s+\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{2}}f_{z_{0}}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{0}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}}_{z})}.

Taking supremum in
z_{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}
and by (
5.3
) and (
5.4
), we obtain

\displaystyle|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|u(t)|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{z}}\lesssim_{C}\int^{t}_{0}|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|u(s)|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{z}}^{2}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}s+|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|f{\mathcal{I}}_{t}|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|^{2}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{0}}_{t}(\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}}_{z})},

which implies by Gronwall’s inequality that for any
t\in[0,1]
,

\sup_{s\in[0,t]}|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|u(s)|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{z}}\lesssim|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|f{\mathcal{I}}_{t}|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{0}}_{t}(\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}}_{z})}.

By this estimate and taking supremum in
z_{0}
for both sides of (
5.7
), we obtain (
5.6
). In particular, (
5.6
) implies the uniqueness of weak solutions.

(
Step 2
). In this step we show the existence of weak solutions. Let
\varGamma_{\varepsilon}
be as in (
3.7
). Define

a_{\varepsilon}:=a*\varGamma_{\varepsilon},\ \ b_{\varepsilon}:=b*\varGamma_{\varepsilon},\ \ f_{\varepsilon}:=f*\varGamma_{\varepsilon}.

Under (
3.2
) and
(
\widetilde{\bf H}^{q_{1}}_{b}
)
, we have

\displaystyle\kappa_{0}{\mathbb{I}}\leqslant a_{\varepsilon}\leqslant\kappa_{1}{\mathbb{I}},\ \ \sup_{\varepsilon\in(0,1)}|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|b_{\varepsilon}|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{1}}_{t}(\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}}_{z})}\leqslant C(\kappa_{2}),

(5.8)

and for each
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
,

a_{\varepsilon},b_{\varepsilon},f_{\varepsilon}\in C^{\infty}_{b}({\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d}).

It is well known that there is a unique smooth solution
u_{\varepsilon}
solving the following PDE:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}u_{\varepsilon}=\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}(a_{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{v}u_{\varepsilon})+v\cdot\nabla_{x}u_{\varepsilon}+b_{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{v}u_{\varepsilon}+f_{\varepsilon},\ \ u_{\varepsilon}(0)=0.

(5.9)

In particular, by Remark
3.2
, for any
\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{c}({\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d})
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d}}u_{\varepsilon}\partial_{t}\varphi&=-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d}}\langle a_{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{v}u_{\varepsilon},\nabla_{v}\varphi\rangle-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d}}u_{\varepsilon}(v\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi)\\
&\quad+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d}}(b_{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{v}u_{\varepsilon})\varphi+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d}}f_{\varepsilon}\varphi.\end{split}

(5.10)

Moreover, by (
5.8
) and (
5.6
), we also have

\displaystyle\sup_{\varepsilon}(\|u_{\varepsilon}{\mathcal{I}}_{1}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}}+|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|u_{\varepsilon}{\mathcal{I}}_{1}|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|_{\widetilde{\mathscr{V}}})<\infty.

(5.11)

By the weak compactness of
\widetilde{\mathscr{V}}
, there are a sequence
\varepsilon_{k}\to 0
and
u\in\widetilde{\mathscr{V}}\cap{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}
such that
for each
\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{c}({\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d})
and
\Phi\in L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d};{\mathbb{R}}^{d})
,

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d}}u_{\varepsilon_{k}}\varphi\to\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d}}u\varphi,\ \ \int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d}}(\varphi\Phi)\cdot\nabla_{v}u_{\varepsilon_{k}}\to\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d}}(\varphi\Phi)\cdot\nabla_{v}u.

Taking limits for both sides of (
5.10
) along
\varepsilon_{k}
, one sees that
u
is a weak solution of PDE (
3.1
).
Let us only show the following limit since the others are completely the same: for
\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{c}({\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d})
,

\lim_{k\to\infty}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d}}(b_{\varepsilon_{k}}\cdot\nabla_{v}u_{\varepsilon_{k}})\varphi=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d}}(b\cdot\nabla_{v}u)\varphi.

Let
Q
be the support of
\varphi
. By Hölder’s inequality and (
5.11
), we have

\displaystyle\left|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d}}((b_{\varepsilon_{k}}-b)\cdot\nabla_{v}u_{\varepsilon_{k}})\varphi\right|

\displaystyle\leqslant\|(b_{\varepsilon_{k}}-b){\mathbf{1}}_{Q}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}\|\nabla_{v}u_{\varepsilon_{k}}{\mathbf{1}}_{Q}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}\|\varphi\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{z}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\|(b_{\varepsilon_{k}}-b){\mathbf{1}}_{Q}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}\to 0,\ k\to\infty,

and by the weak convergence of
\nabla_{v}u_{\varepsilon_{k}}
,

\lim_{k\to\infty}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d}}b\cdot\nabla_{v}(u_{\varepsilon_{k}}-u)\,\varphi=0.

Thus we complete the proof.
∎

Now we present a stability result which shall be used to show the well-posedness of generalized martingale problem when the coefficients are bounded measurable.

Theorem 5.2
.

Let
(a_{n},b_{n},f_{n})
be a sequence of coefficients that satisfy the following assumptions:

(i)

a_{n}
satisfies (
3.2
) uniformly in
n
and
b_{n}
,
f_{n}
are uniformly bounded.

(ii)

(a_{n},b_{n},f_{n})
converges to
(a,b,f)
in Lebesgue measure as
n\to\infty
.

Let
u_{n}
and
u
be the respective weak solutions of PDE (
3.1
) corresponding to
(a_{n},b_{n},f_{n})
and
(a,b,f)
with zero initial value.
Then for any bounded domain
Q\subset{\mathbb{R}}_{+}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}
,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{(t,z)\in Q}|u_{n}(t,z)-u(t,z)|=0.

(5.12)

Proof.

First of all, by (i) and (
5.6
) we have for any
T>0
,

\displaystyle\sup_{n}(\|u_{n}{\mathcal{I}}_{T}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}}+|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|u_{n}{\mathcal{I}}_{T}|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|_{\widetilde{\mathscr{V}}})<\infty.

(5.13)

By Remark
3.2
, one can extend
u_{n}
to a global weak solution in
{\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d}
.
Thus by (i) and
[
16
, Theorem 3]
, there are
\alpha\in(0,1)
and constant
C>0
such that for any
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
and all
(t,z),(t^{\prime},z^{\prime})\in Q_{1/2}
,

|u_{n}(t,z)-u_{n}(t^{\prime},z^{\prime})|\leqslant C|(t,z)-(t^{\prime},z^{\prime})|^{\alpha}(\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{1}}u_{n}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}+\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{1}}f_{n}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}}),

which, together with (
5.13
) and a shifting argument as used in
Step 1
of Theorem
5.1
, yields that for any bounded domain
Q\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d}
,
there is a constant
C>0
such that for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
and
(t,z),(t^{\prime},z^{\prime})\in Q
,

|u_{n}(t,z)-u_{n}(t^{\prime},z^{\prime})|\leqslant C|(t,z)-(t^{\prime},z^{\prime})|^{\alpha}.

In particular, by (
5.13
), the weak compactness of
\widetilde{\mathscr{V}}
and Ascolli-Arzela’s theorem,
there are a subsequence
n_{k}
and
\bar{u}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{V}}\cap C({\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d})
such that for any
\Phi\in L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d};{\mathbb{R}}^{d})
with compact support,

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d}}\Phi\cdot\nabla_{v}u_{n_{k}}\to\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d}}\Phi\cdot\nabla_{v}\bar{u},

and for any bounded domain
Q\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d}
,

\lim_{k\to\infty}\sup_{(t,z)\in Q}|u_{n_{k}}(t,z)-\bar{u}(t,z)|=0.

As in
Step 2
of Theorem
5.1
, by (ii) one sees that
\bar{u}=u
is the unique weak solution of PDE (
3.1
).
By a contradiction method, one sees that (
5.12
) holds for the whole sequence.
∎

Below we assume that
a\in L^{\infty}_{T}(C^{\infty}_{b}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d}))
satisfies (
3.2
) and

\displaystyle b_{1},b_{2}\in L^{\infty}_{T}(C^{\infty}_{b}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})),\ g\in L^{\infty}_{T}(C^{\infty}_{c}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})),\ \varphi\in C^{\infty}_{b}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d}).

(5.14)

For applications in SDEs with density dependent coefficients, we need the following apriori
L^{\infty}
-estimate for the Cauchy problem of the following kinetic FPKE:

\partial_{t}u=\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}(a\cdot\nabla_{v}u)+v\cdot\nabla_{x}u+b_{1}\cdot\nabla_{v}u+\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}(b_{2}u)+g,\ \ u(0)=\varphi.

Note that under (
5.14
), it is well known that there is a unique smooth solution to the above PDE.

Theorem 5.3
.

Suppose that for some
q_{i}\in(2,4)
and
{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i}\in(2,\infty)^{2d}
with
\tfrac{2}{q_{i}}+{\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i}}<1
,
i=1,2
,

|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|b_{1}|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{1}}_{t}(\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}}_{z})}+|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|b_{2}|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{2}}_{t}(\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{2}}_{z})}\leqslant\kappa_{2}.

If
(q_{0},{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d}
satisfies (
4.19
) for some
\alpha_{0}\in[0,1)
, then for any
T>0
,
there is a constant
C>0
only depending on
T,\alpha_{0},\kappa_{i},q_{i},{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i}
such that for all
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\|u{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}}\lesssim_{C}|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|g{\mathcal{I}}_{t}|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{0}}_{t}(\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{-\alpha_{0}}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}+\|\varphi\|_{C^{1}_{b}}.

(5.15)

Proof.

First of all, let
\bar{u}:=P_{t}\varphi
, where
P_{t}
is defined by (
3.14
). Note that

\partial_{t}\bar{u}=\Delta_{v}\bar{u}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}\bar{u},\ \ \bar{u}(0)=\varphi.

It is easy to see that
\tilde{u}:=u-\bar{u}
solves the following PDE:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\tilde{u}=\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}(a\cdot\nabla_{v}\tilde{u})+v\cdot\nabla_{x}\tilde{u}+b_{1}\cdot\nabla_{v}\tilde{u}+\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}G+\tilde{g}+g,\ \ \tilde{u}(0)=0,

(5.16)

where

G:=(a-{\mathbb{I}})\cdot\nabla_{v}\bar{u}+b_{2}u,\ \ \tilde{g}:=b_{1}\cdot\nabla_{v}\bar{u}.

Below we consider PDE (
5.16
) with zero initial value.
Since
q_{1}\in(2,4)
and
{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}\in(2,\infty)^{2d}
satisfy
\tfrac{2}{q_{1}}+{\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}}<1
, one can choose

q_{3}\in(1,\frac{4}{3})
and
{\boldsymbol{p}}_{3}\in(1,\infty)^{2d}
so that

{\boldsymbol{p}}_{3}\leqslant{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1},\ \ \tfrac{2}{q_{3}}+{\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{3}}<2.

Suppose
T=1
. As in the
Step 1
of Theorem
5.1
, by applying Theorem
4.5
to
\tilde{u}^{+}_{z_{0}}
and
\tilde{u}^{-}_{z_{0}}
(see Remark
4.7
),
there is a constant
C=C(\kappa_{i},q_{i},{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i})>0
such that for each
t\in[0,1]
and
z_{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{1}}\tilde{u}_{z_{0}}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}}+\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{1}}\nabla_{v}\tilde{u}_{z_{0}}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}&\lesssim_{C}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{2}}\tilde{u}_{z_{0}}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}+{\mathcal{G}}^{t}_{z_{0}},\end{split}

(5.17)

where

{\mathcal{G}}^{t}_{z_{0}}:=\|g_{z_{0}}\chi_{2}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{0}}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{-\alpha_{0}}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}+\|\tilde{g}_{z_{0}}\chi_{2}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{3}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{3}}_{z})}+\|G_{z_{0}}\chi_{2}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{2}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{2}}_{z})}.

In particular, for each
t\in[0,1]
,

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{1}}\tilde{u}_{z_{0}}(t)\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{z}}

\displaystyle\leqslant{\rm Vol}(Q_{1})\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{1}}\tilde{u}_{z_{0}}(t)\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{z}}^{2}\lesssim\int^{t}_{0}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{2}}\tilde{u}_{z_{0}}(s)\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{z}}^{2}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}s+({\mathcal{G}}^{t}_{z_{0}})^{2}.

Taking supremum in
z_{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}
and by (
5.3
), we obtain

\displaystyle|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|\tilde{u}(t)|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{z}}\lesssim\int^{t}_{0}|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|\tilde{u}(s)|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{z}}^{2}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}s+\sup_{z_{0}}({\mathcal{G}}^{t}_{z_{0}})^{2},

which implies by Gronwall’s inequality that for any
t\in[0,1]
,

\sup_{s\in[0,t]}|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|\tilde{u}(s)|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{z}}\lesssim\sup_{z_{0}}{\mathcal{G}}^{t}_{z_{0}}.

Substituting it into (
5.17
) and taking supremum in
z_{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}
, we get

\displaystyle\|u{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}}\leqslant\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\|P_{s}\varphi\|_{\infty}+\|\tilde{u}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}}\lesssim_{C}\|\varphi\|_{\infty}+\sup_{z_{0}}{\mathcal{G}}^{t}_{z_{0}}.

(5.18)

By
\|\nabla_{v}\bar{u}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}}=\sup_{t}\|\nabla_{v}P_{t}\varphi\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{z}}\lesssim\|\varphi\|_{C^{1}_{b}}
, and
{\boldsymbol{p}}_{3}\leqslant{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}
and
q_{3}\leqslant q_{1}
,

\sup_{z_{0}}\|\tilde{g}_{z_{0}}\chi_{2}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{3}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{3}}_{z})}\leqslant|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|b_{1}|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{3}}_{t}(\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{3}}_{z})}\|\nabla_{v}\bar{u}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}}\lesssim|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|b_{1}|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{1}}_{t}(\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}}_{z})}\|\varphi\|_{C^{1}_{b}},

and

\displaystyle\sup_{z_{0}}\|G_{z_{0}}\chi_{2}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{2}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{2}}_{z})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla_{v}\bar{u}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}}+\sup_{z_{0}}\|(b_{2}u)_{z_{0}}\chi_{2}{\mathcal{I}}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{2}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{2}}_{z})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\varphi\|_{C^{1}_{b}}+\left(\int^{t}_{0}|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|b_{2}(s)|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|^{q_{2}}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{2}}_{z}}\|u(s)\|^{q_{2}}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{z}}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}s\right)^{1/q_{2}}.

Substituting the above estimates into (
5.18
), we get for any
t\in[0,1]
,

\displaystyle\|u(t)\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{z}}\lesssim_{C}\|\varphi\|_{C^{1}_{b}}+|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|g{\mathcal{I}}_{t}|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{0}}_{t}(\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{-\alpha_{0}}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}+\left(\int^{t}_{0}|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|b_{2}(s)|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|^{q_{2}}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{2}}_{z}}\|u(s)\|^{q_{2}}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{z}}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}s\right)^{1/q_{2}},

which implies the desired estimate (
5.15
) by Gronwall’s inequality.
∎

6.
Weak solutions of second order McKean-Vlasov SDEs

In this section we devote to showing the existence of weak solutions to the following second order McKean-Vlasov SDE:

\displaystyle{\mathord{{\rm d}}}X_{t}=V_{t}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}t,\ \ {\mathord{{\rm d}}}V_{t}=b_{Z}(t,Z_{t}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}t+\sigma_{Z}(t,X_{t}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}W_{t},

(6.1)

where
Z_{t}=(X_{t},V_{t})
and
b_{Z},\sigma_{Z}
are defined by (
1.2
) and (
1.3
), respectively.

Let
{\mathbb{C}}
be the space of all continuous functions from
{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
to
{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}
, which is endowed with
the locally uniformly convergence topology. Let
\omega_{t}
be the canonical process on
{\mathbb{C}}
, and
{\mathcal{B}}_{t}
the natural filtration associated with
\omega_{t}
.
Let
{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{C}})
be the space of all probability measures over
{\mathbb{C}}
.

The notion of weak solutions has been introduced in Definition
1.1
.
Now we introduce the equivalent notion of classical martingale solutions (cf.
[
31
]
).
The advantage of using martingale solutions is that it avoids the use of stochastic integrals.

Definition 6.1
.

Let
\nu\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})
. We call a probability measure
{\mathbb{P}}\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{C}})
a classical martingale solution of DDSDE (
6.1
) with initial distribution
\nu
if

(i)

{\mathbb{P}}\circ\omega^{-1}_{0}=\nu
, and for Lebesgue almost all
t\geqslant 0
,
{\mathbb{P}}\circ\omega_{t}^{-1}({\mathord{{\rm d}}}z)=\rho_{t}(z){\mathord{{\rm d}}}z
.

(ii)

For each
f\in C^{2}_{c}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})
, the process

\displaystyle M_{t}(\omega):=f(\omega_{t})-f(\omega_{0})-\int^{t}_{0}\left(\mathrm{tr}(a_{\rho}\cdot\nabla^{2}_{v}f)+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f+b_{\rho}\cdot\nabla_{v}f\right)(r,\omega_{r}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}r

(6.2)

is a
{\mathcal{B}}_{t}
-martingale with respect to
{\mathbb{P}}
, where for
\langle\rho_{t}\rangle(x):=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rho_{t}(x,v){\mathord{{\rm d}}}v
,

\displaystyle a_{\rho}(t,x)

\displaystyle:=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}a(t,x,\langle\rho_{t}\rangle(x),z^{\prime})\rho_{t}(z^{\prime}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}z^{\prime},

(6.3)

\displaystyle b_{\rho}(t,z)

\displaystyle:=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}b(t,z,\rho_{t}(z),z^{\prime})\rho_{t}(z^{\prime}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}z^{\prime}.

(6.4)

The set of all the martingale solutions
{\mathbb{P}}\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{C}})
with initial distribution
\nu
is denoted by
{\mathscr{M}}^{a,b}_{\nu}
.

Remark 6.2
.

Note that
a_{\rho}
and
b_{\rho}
are only well defined except for some Lebesgue null set.
This does not affect the definition of (
6.2
) due to the point (i).
Under (
1.4
), it is well known that weak solution in Definition
1.1
is equivalent to the above martingale solution (see
[
31
]
).

Our main result of this section is to show the following existence result.

Theorem 6.3
.

Under
(H
1
)
and
(H
2
)
, for any initial probability measure
\nu\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})
, there exists at least one classical martingale solution
{\mathbb{P}}\in{\mathscr{M}}^{a,b}_{\nu}

in the sense of Definition
6.1
. Moreover,
\rho
enjoys the regularity (
1.11
).

To show the existence of a martingale solution, by mollifying the coefficients and the fact that
a_{\rho}
does not depend on
v
,
the key point is to use (
5.6
) and (
5.15
) to establish the uniform Krylov estimate for approximating DDSDEs.
For
N\in{\mathbb{N}}
, let
\varGamma\in C^{\infty}_{c}({\mathbb{R}}^{N})
be a probability density function and define

\varGamma_{n}(\cdot)=n^{N}\varGamma(n\cdot),\ \ n\in{\mathbb{N}}.

With a little confusion, we shall use the same notation
\varGamma_{n}
to denote different mollifiers for different dimension
N
.
For
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, define

\displaystyle b_{n}(t,z,r,z^{\prime})

\displaystyle:=(b(t,\cdot,\cdot,\cdot)*\varGamma_{n})(z,r,z^{\prime}),

\displaystyle a_{n}(t,x,r,z^{\prime})

\displaystyle:=(a(t,\cdot,\cdot,\cdot)*\varGamma_{n})(x,r,z^{\prime}),

and for a probability measure
\mu\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})
,

\displaystyle b^{n}_{\mu}(t,z)

\displaystyle:=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}b_{n}(t,z,\mu*\varGamma_{n}(z),z^{\prime})\mu({\mathord{{\rm d}}}z^{\prime}),

\displaystyle a^{n}_{\mu}(t,x)

\displaystyle:=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}a_{n}(t,x,\mu*\varGamma_{n}(x),z^{\prime})\mu({\mathord{{\rm d}}}z^{\prime}).

For two probability measures
\mu_{1},\mu_{2}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})

with finite second order moments, the Wasserstein metric is defined by

{\mathcal{W}}_{2}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2}):=\inf\left\{\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{4d}}|z-z^{\prime}|^{2}\pi({\mathord{{\rm d}}}z,{\mathord{{\rm d}}}z^{\prime}):\pi\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{R}}^{4d}),\ \pi(\cdot,{\mathbb{R}}^{2d})=\mu_{1},\pi({\mathbb{R}}^{2d},\cdot)=\mu_{2}\right\}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

The following lemma provides necessary estimates for
b^{n}_{\mu}
and
a^{n}_{\mu}
.

Lemma 6.4
.

Under
(H
1
)
and
(H
2
)
, for each
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
T>0
and
\mu\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})
, we have

\displaystyle b^{n}_{\mu}\in L^{\infty}_{T}C^{\infty}_{b}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d}),\ \ a^{n}_{\mu}\in L^{\infty}_{T}C^{\infty}_{b}({\mathbb{R}}^{d}),

(6.5)

and there is a constant
C=C(T,n)>0
such that for any
\mu_{1},\mu_{2}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})
,

\displaystyle\|b^{n}_{\mu_{1}}-b^{n}_{\mu_{2}}\|_{\infty}+\|\sqrt{a^{n}_{\mu_{1}}}-\sqrt{a^{n}_{\mu_{2}}}\|_{\infty}\lesssim_{C}{\mathcal{W}}_{2}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2}).

(6.6)

Moreover, we also have

\displaystyle\sup_{n}|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|b^{n}_{\mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{1}}_{t}(\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}}_{z})}\leqslant|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|h|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{1}}_{t}(\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}}_{z})}\leqslant\kappa_{2},

(6.7)

and for all
\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
and
(t,x)\in{\mathbb{R}}_{+}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\kappa_{0}|\xi|^{2}\leqslant\xi\cdot a^{n}_{\mu}(t,x)\xi\leqslant\kappa_{1}|\xi|^{2}.

(6.8)

Proof.

Estimates (
6.5
) and (
6.8
) are obvious by definition. For (
6.7
), noting that by (
1.9
),

\displaystyle|b^{n}_{\mu}(t,z)|

\displaystyle\leqslant\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}\left(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{4d}}h(t,z-z_{1}-z^{\prime}+z^{\prime}_{1})\varGamma_{n}(z_{1},z_{1}^{\prime}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}z_{1}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}z_{1}^{\prime}\right)\mu({\mathord{{\rm d}}}z^{\prime}),

by Minkowskii’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|b^{n}_{\mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{1}}_{t}(\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}}_{z})}

\displaystyle\leqslant\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}\left(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{4d}}|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|h|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{1}}_{t}(\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}}_{z})}\varGamma_{n}(z_{1},z_{1}^{\prime}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}z_{1}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}z_{1}^{\prime}\right)\mu({\mathord{{\rm d}}}z^{\prime})=|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|h|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{1}}_{t}(\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}}_{z})}.

Below we prove the second estimate in (
6.6
), and divide the proofs into three steps.

(
Step 1
) In this step we show the following claim: Suppose that
A(r):{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbb{M}}^{d}_{\rm sym}
is a
C^{1}
-positive
definite matrix-valued function so that
A(r)\geqslant\kappa_{0}{\mathbb{I}}
for all
r
. Then (cf.
[
31
, Theorem 5.2.2]
)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{r}A^{\frac{1}{2}}(r)\|_{HS}\leqslant\|\partial_{r}A(r)\|_{HS}/(2\sqrt{\kappa_{0}}).

(6.9)

Indeed, by the chain rule, we have

\partial_{r}A(r)=\partial_{r}A^{\frac{1}{2}}(r)\cdot A^{\frac{1}{2}}(r)+A^{\frac{1}{2}}(r)\cdot\partial_{r}A^{\frac{1}{2}}(r).

Let
P
be an orthogonal matrix so that
PA^{\frac{1}{2}}(r)P^{*}=:D
is an diagonal matrix, where the asterisk stands for the transpose of a matrix.
Then

P\partial_{r}A(r)P^{*}=(P\partial_{r}A^{\frac{1}{2}}(r)P^{*})D+D(P\partial_{r}A^{\frac{1}{2}}(r)P^{*})

and

(P\partial_{r}A^{\frac{1}{2}}(r)P^{*})_{ij}=(P\partial_{r}A(r)P^{*})_{ij}/(D_{jj}+D_{ii}).

From this we derive (
6.9
).

(
Step 2
) Let
{\mathbb{H}}
be a separable Hilbert space and
A:{\mathbb{H}}\to{\mathbb{M}}^{d}_{\rm sym}
a
C^{1}
-positive
definite matrix-valued function. Suppose that for all
h,h^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{H}}
,

A(h)\geqslant\kappa_{0}{\mathbb{I}},\ \|A(h)-A(h^{\prime})\|_{HS}\leqslant\kappa_{1}\|h-h^{\prime}\|_{\mathbb{H}}.

Then it holds that

\displaystyle\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}(h)-A^{\frac{1}{2}}(h^{\prime})\|_{HS}\leqslant\kappa_{1}\|h-h^{\prime}\|_{\mathbb{H}}/(2\sqrt{\kappa_{0}}).

(6.10)

Indeed, without loss of generality, we may assume
{\mathbb{H}}=\ell^{2}
, where
\ell^{2}
is the usual sequence Hilbert space.
For each
h=(h_{1},h_{2},\cdots)\in\ell^{2}
, the assumption implies that

\sum_{j\in{\mathbb{N}}}\|\partial_{h_{j}}A(h)\|^{2}_{HS}\leqslant\kappa_{1}^{2}.

Thus, by Hölder’s inequality and (
6.9
), we have for some
{\bf h}^{*}_{j}\in\ell^{2}
,

\displaystyle\|A^{\frac{1}{2}}(h)-A^{\frac{1}{2}}(h^{\prime})\|_{HS}

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{j\in{\mathbb{N}}}|h_{j}-h_{j}^{\prime}|\|(\partial_{h_{j}}A^{\frac{1}{2}})({\bf h}^{*}_{j})\|_{HS}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|h-h^{\prime}\|_{\mathbb{H}}\left(\sum_{j\in{\mathbb{N}}}\|(\partial_{h_{j}}A^{\frac{1}{2}})({\bf h}^{*}_{j})\|^{2}_{HS}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{\|h-h^{\prime}\|_{\mathbb{H}}}{2\sqrt{\kappa_{0}}}\left(\sum_{j\in{\mathbb{N}}}\|(\partial_{h_{j}}A)({\bf h}^{*}_{j})\|^{2}_{HS}\right)^{1/2}\leqslant\frac{\kappa_{1}\|h-h^{\prime}\|_{\mathbb{H}}}{2\sqrt{\kappa_{0}}}.

(
Step 3
) For simplicity, we drop the variable
(t,x)
. Suppose that
X\in L^{2}(\Omega,{\mathbf{P}})
has distribution
\mu
.
Then we can write

a^{n}_{\mu}=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}a_{n}(\mu*\varGamma_{n},z^{\prime})\mu({\mathord{{\rm d}}}z^{\prime})={\mathbf{E}}a_{n}(\mu*\varGamma_{n},X)=:A(\mu*\varGamma_{n},X).

Thus for any
X,Y\in L^{2}(\Omega,{\mathbf{P}})
with distributions
\mu_{1}
and
\mu_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{a^{n}_{\mu_{1}}}-\sqrt{a^{n}_{\mu_{2}}}=\sqrt{A(\mu_{1}*\varGamma_{n},X)}-\sqrt{A(\mu_{2}*\varGamma_{n},Y)},

and by (
6.9
) and (
6.10
),

\displaystyle\|\sqrt{a^{n}_{\mu_{1}}}-\sqrt{a^{n}_{\mu_{2}}}\|_{HS}

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{n}(|\mu_{1}*\varGamma_{n}-\mu_{2}*\varGamma_{n}|+\|X-Y\|_{L^{2}})

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{n}({\mathbf{E}}|\varGamma_{n}(\cdot-X)-\varGamma_{n}(\cdot-Y)|+\|X-Y\|_{L^{2}})

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{n}\|X-Y\|_{L^{2}},

which in turn implies that

\|\sqrt{a^{n}_{\mu_{1}}}-\sqrt{a^{n}_{\mu_{2}}}\|_{HS}\leqslant C_{n}{\mathcal{W}}_{2}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2}).

The proof is complete.
∎

Now let
Z_{0}
be an
{\mathscr{F}}_{0}
-measurable random variable with distribution
\nu
. Let us consider the following approximating DDSDE:

\displaystyle{\mathord{{\rm d}}}X^{n}_{t}=V^{n}_{t}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}t,\ \ {\mathord{{\rm d}}}V^{n}_{t}=b^{n}_{\mu_{Z^{n}_{t}}}(t,Z^{n}_{t}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}t+\sqrt{a^{n}_{\mu_{Z^{n}_{t}}}}(t,X^{n}_{t}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}W_{t},

(6.11)

subject to the initial value

Z^{n}_{0}:=(-n)\vee Z_{0}\wedge n,

where for a vector
z\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}
,
((-n)\vee z\wedge n)_{i}:=(-n)\vee z_{i}\wedge n
.

By Lemma
6.4
and standard Picard’s iteration,
it is well known that the above SDE admits a unique strong solution
Z^{n}_{t}
.
Now we use Theorems
5.1
and
5.3
to derive the following crucial Krylov estimates.

Lemma 6.5

(Krylov’s estimates)
.

Let
\alpha_{0}\in[0,1)
and
(q_{0},{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0})\in(1,\infty)^{1+2d}
satisfy (
4.19
).
For any
T>0
, there are
\theta\in(0,1)
and constant
C>0
depending only on
\kappa_{i}

and
q_{i},{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i}
such that for any
\delta\in(0,1)
, stopping time
\tau\leqslant T
and
f\in C^{\infty}_{c}({\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d})
,

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}{\mathbf{E}}\left(\int^{\tau+\delta}_{\tau}f(r,Z^{n}_{r}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}r\Big|{\mathscr{F}}_{\tau}\right)\lesssim_{C}\delta^{\theta}|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|{\mathbf{1}}_{[0,T]}f|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{0}}_{t}(\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{-\alpha_{0}}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}\lesssim_{C}\delta^{\theta}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{[0,T]}f\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{0}}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{-\alpha_{0}}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}.

(6.12)

Proof.

Fix
T>0
. By Lemma
6.4
, we have

b^{n}_{\mu_{Z^{n}_{t}}}\in L^{\infty}_{T}C^{\infty}_{b}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d}),\ \ a^{n}_{\mu_{Z^{n}_{t}}}\in L^{\infty}_{T}C^{\infty}_{b}({\mathbb{R}}^{d}).

Fix
0\leqslant t_{0}<t_{1}\leqslant T
and
f\in C^{\infty}_{c}({\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d})
. Let
u_{n}\in L^{\infty}_{t_{1}}(C^{\infty}_{b}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d}))
solve the following backward Kolmogorov equation:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}u_{n}+\mathrm{tr}\big(a^{n}_{\mu_{Z^{n}_{t}}}\cdot\nabla^{2}_{v}u_{n}\big)+v\cdot\nabla_{x}u_{n}+b^{n}_{\mu_{Z^{n}_{t}}}\cdot\nabla_{v}u_{n}=f,\ \ u_{n}(t_{1})=0.

(6.13)

Since
{\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}}+\tfrac{2}{q_{0}}<2
, one can choose
\bar{q}_{0}<q_{0}
so that

{\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}}+\tfrac{2}{\bar{q}_{0}}<2.

Thus by (
6.7
), (
6.8
), (
1.14
) and (
5.15
), there is a constant
C=C(\kappa_{i},q_{i},{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i})>0
such that
