\displaystyle\sup_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{[t_{0},t_{1}]}u_{n}\|_{\infty}\lesssim_{C}|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|{\mathbf{1}}_{[t_{0},t_{1}]}f|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{\bar{q}_{0}}_{t}(\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{-\alpha_{0}}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}\lesssim_{C}(t_{1}-t_{0})^{\frac{1}{\bar{q}_{0}}-\frac{1}{q_{0}}}|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|{\mathbf{1}}_{[t_{0},t_{1}]}f|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{0}}_{t}(\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{-\alpha_{0}}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0};{\boldsymbol{a}}})},

(6.14)

where the second step is due to Hölder’s inequality.
Now by (
6.13
) and Itô’s formula, we have

\displaystyle 0=u_{n}(t_{1},Z^{n}_{t_{1}})=u_{n}(t_{0},Z^{n}_{t_{0}})+\int^{t_{1}}_{t_{0}}f(r,Z^{n}_{r}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}r+\int^{t_{1}}_{t_{0}}\Big\langle\nabla_{v}u_{n}(r,Z^{n}_{r}),\sqrt{a^{n}_{\mu_{Z^{n}_{r}}}}(r,X^{n}_{r}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}W_{r}\Big\rangle.

Hence, by taking conditional expectation with respect to
{\mathscr{F}}_{t_{0}}
and (
6.14
),

\displaystyle{\mathbf{E}}\left(\int^{t_{1}}_{t_{0}}f(r,Z^{n}_{r}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}r\Big|{\mathscr{F}}_{t_{0}}\right)=-u_{n}(t_{0},Z^{n}_{t_{0}})\lesssim(t_{1}-t_{0})^{\frac{1}{\bar{q}_{0}}-\frac{1}{q_{0}}}|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|{\mathbf{1}}_{[t_{0},t_{1}]}f|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{0}}_{t}(\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{-\alpha_{0}}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}.

By discretization stopping time approximation (see
[
40
, Remark 1.2]
), we obtain the first estimate in (
6.12
).
Note that by Lemma
2.5
,

|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|{\mathbf{1}}_{[0,T]}f|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{0}}_{t}(\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{-\alpha_{0}}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}\lesssim\sup_{z_{0}}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{[0,T]}f(\cdot-z_{0})\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{0}}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{-\alpha_{0}}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}\|\chi_{2}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{1}_{\infty;{\boldsymbol{a}}})}\lesssim\|{\mathbf{1}}_{[0,T]}f\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{0}}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{-\alpha_{0}}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}.

The second estimate in (
6.12
) then follows.
∎

Remark 6.6
.

Under
(
\widetilde{\bf H}_{b}
)
, one can use Theorem
5.1
to obtain the following Krylov estimate: for any
(q_{0},p_{0})\in(1,\infty)^{1+2d}

satisfying (
4.4
),

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}{\mathbf{E}}\left(\int^{\tau+\delta}_{\tau}f(r,Z^{n}_{r}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}r\Big|{\mathscr{F}}_{\tau}\right)\leqslant C\delta^{\theta}|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|{\mathbf{1}}_{[0,T]}f|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{0}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}}_{z})}.

(6.15)

The following corollary is direct by (
6.12
).

Corollary 6.7
.

For Lebesgue almost all
t\geqslant 0
,
Z^{n}_{t}
admits a density
\rho^{n}_{t}(z)
with the following regularity: for any
\alpha\in[0,1)
and
(q,{\boldsymbol{p}})\in(1,\infty)^{1+2d}
satisfying

\tfrac{2}{q}<1+\alpha,\ \ \ \tfrac{2}{q}+{\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot(\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}-{\mathbf{1}})>2\alpha,

it holds that

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}\|\rho^{n}{\mathbf{1}}_{[0,T]}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{\alpha}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}<\infty,\ T>0.

(6.16)

Proof.

Let
\mu^{n}_{t}({\mathord{{\rm d}}}z)
be the probability distribution of
Z^{n}_{t}
. By Krylov’s estimate (
6.12
),
for any
\alpha_{0}\in[0,1)
and
(q_{0},{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0})\in(1,\infty)^{1+2d}
satisfying (
4.19
), there is a constant
C_{T}>0
such that for any
f\in C^{\infty}_{c}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})
,

\sup_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}\int^{T}_{0}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}f(r,z)\mu^{n}_{t}({\mathord{{\rm d}}}z){\mathord{{\rm d}}}r\leqslant C_{T}\|{\mathbf{1}}_{[0,T]}f\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{0}}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{-\alpha_{0}}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}.

This implies that
\mu^{n}_{t}
has a density
\rho^{n}_{t}(z)
, which belongs to the dual space of
L^{q_{0}}([0,T];{\mathbf{B}}^{-\alpha_{0}}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0};{\boldsymbol{a}}})
. In particular, we have
(
6.16
).
∎

By Krylov’s estimate (
6.12
), we can also show the following tightness result.

Lemma 6.8
.

The law
{\mathbb{P}}_{n}
of
Z^{n}
in
{\mathbb{C}}
is tight. Moreover, for any accumulation point
{\mathbb{P}}
, it holds that
{\mathbb{P}}\circ\omega_{t}^{-1}({\mathord{{\rm d}}}z)=\rho_{t}(z){\mathord{{\rm d}}}z
,
where
\rho
satisfies the same estimate (
6.16
).

Proof.

Fix
T>0
. Let
\tau
be any stopping time bounded by
T
. By SDE (
6.11
), we have

\displaystyle V^{n}_{\tau+t}-V^{n}_{\tau}=\int^{\tau+t}_{\tau}b^{n}_{\mu_{Z^{n}_{r}}}(r,Z^{n}_{r}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}r+\int^{\tau+t}_{\tau}\sqrt{a^{n}_{\mu_{Z^{n}_{r}}}}(r,X^{n}_{r}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}W_{r},

and

\displaystyle X^{n}_{\tau+t}-X^{n}_{\tau}=\int^{\tau+t}_{\tau}V^{n}_{r}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}r.

(6.17)

By Krylov’s estimate (
6.12
) and (
6.7
), there is a
\theta\in(0,1)
such that for all
\delta\in(0,1)
and
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,

\displaystyle{\mathbf{E}}\left(\int^{\tau+\delta}_{\tau}|b^{n}_{\mu_{Z^{n}_{t}}}(r,Z^{n}_{r})|{\mathord{{\rm d}}}r\right)\lesssim\delta^{\theta}|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|b^{n}_{\mu_{Z^{n}_{r}}}|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{1}}_{t}(\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}}_{z})}\lesssim\delta^{\theta}|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|h|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{1}}_{t}(\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}}_{z})},

and by BDG’s inequality and (
6.8
),

\displaystyle{\mathbf{E}}\left|\sup_{t\in[0,\delta]}\int^{\tau+t}_{\tau}\sqrt{a^{n}_{\mu_{Z^{n}_{r}}}}(r,X^{n}_{r}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}W_{r}\right|\lesssim{\mathbf{E}}\left(\int^{\tau+\delta}_{\tau}\big\|a^{n}_{\mu_{Z^{n}_{r}}}(r,X^{n}_{r})\big\|_{HS}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}r\right)^{1/2}\lesssim\delta^{1/2},

where the implicit constant is independent of
n
,
\tau
and
\delta\in(0,1)
.
Hence,

\displaystyle\sup_{n}{\mathbf{E}}\left(\sup_{t\in[0,\delta]}|V^{n}_{\tau+t}-V^{n}_{\tau}|\right)\lesssim\delta^{\theta\wedge(1/2)}.

(6.18)

Noting that for any
R>0
,

{\mathbf{P}}\left(\sup_{t\in[0,T+\delta]}|V^{n}_{t}|\geqslant R\right)\leqslant{\mathbf{P}}\left(\sup_{t\in[0,T+\delta]}|V^{n}_{t}-V^{n}_{0}|\geqslant\tfrac{R}{2}\right)+{\mathbf{P}}\left(|V^{n}_{0}|\geqslant\tfrac{R}{2}\right),

and by
|V^{n}_{0}|\leqslant|V_{0}|
, (
6.18
) and Chebyschev’s inequality, we get

\displaystyle\lim_{R\to\infty}\sup_{n}{\mathbf{P}}\left(\sup_{t\in[0,T+\delta]}|V^{n}_{t}|\geqslant R\right)=0.

(6.19)

Thus, by (
6.17
) and (
6.19
), we derive that for any
\varepsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta\to 0}\sup_{n}{\mathbf{P}}\left(\sup_{t\in[0,\delta]}|X^{n}_{\tau+t}-X^{n}_{\tau}|>\varepsilon\right)=0,

which, together with (
6.18
) and (
6.19
), and by
[
31
, Theorem 1.3.2]
, yields the tightness of
{\mathbb{P}}_{n}
.

Let
{\mathbb{P}}
be any accumulation point. By Prohorov’s theorem, there is a subsequence
n_{m}
such that
{\mathbb{P}}_{n_{m}}
weakly converges to
{\mathbb{P}}
as
m\to\infty
.
By taking weak limits, one sees that for any
T>0
,
f\in C^{\infty}_{c}({\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d})
and any
\alpha_{0}\in[0,1)
and
(q_{0},{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0})\in(1,\infty)^{1+2d}
satisfying (
4.19
),

{\mathbb{E}}^{\mathbb{P}}\left(\int^{T}_{0}f(r,\omega_{r}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}r\right)\lesssim\|f\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{0}}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{-\alpha_{0}}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}.

As in Corollary
6.7
,
{\mathbb{P}}\circ\omega_{t}^{-1}({\mathord{{\rm d}}}z)=\rho_{t}(z){\mathord{{\rm d}}}z
and
\rho
satisfies (
6.16
).
∎

The following lemma provides the strong convergence of the density.

Lemma 6.9
.

For fixed accumulation point
{\mathbb{P}}
of
({\mathbb{P}}_{n})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
, there is a subsequence
n_{k}
such that
{\mathbb{P}}_{n_{k}}
weakly converges to
{\mathbb{P}}

as
k\to\infty
, and for any bounded domain
Q\subset{\mathbb{R}}_{+}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}
,

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\|(\rho^{n_{k}}-\rho){\mathbf{1}}_{Q}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{1}_{z})}=0,

(6.20)

where
{\mathbb{P}}^{n_{k}}\circ\omega_{t}^{-1}({\mathord{{\rm d}}}z)=\rho^{n_{k}}_{t}(z){\mathord{{\rm d}}}z
and
{\mathbb{P}}\circ\omega_{t}^{-1}({\mathord{{\rm d}}}z)=\rho_{t}(z){\mathord{{\rm d}}}z
.

Proof.

First of all, by Lemma
6.8
, there is a subsequence
n_{m}
so that
{\mathbb{P}}_{n_{m}}
weakly converges to
{\mathbb{P}}
as
m\to\infty
.
Next we look at (
6.20
). Note that
\rho^{n}_{t}(z)
solves the following FPKE:

\partial_{t}\rho^{n}_{t}=\partial_{v_{i}}\partial_{v_{j}}\big((a^{n}_{\mu_{Z^{n}_{t}}})_{ij}\rho^{n}_{t}\big)-v\cdot\nabla_{x}\rho^{n}_{t}+\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}\big(b^{n}_{\mu_{Z^{n}_{t}}}\rho^{n}_{t}\big).

Given
R\geqslant 1
, let
\chi_{R}
be the cutoff function defined in (
3.21
). Since
\chi_{2R}\equiv 1
on the support of
\chi_{R}
,
by Bernstein’s inequality (
2.9
), it is easy to see that

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}(\rho^{n}\chi_{R})\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{-3}_{1;a})}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\partial_{v_{i}}\partial_{v_{j}}\big(a^{n}_{\mu_{Z^{n}_{t}}}\rho^{n}\chi_{2R}\big)\chi_{R}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{-3}_{1;a})}+\|v\cdot\nabla_{x}(\rho^{n}\chi_{2R})\chi_{R}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{-3}_{1;a})}

\displaystyle\quad+\|\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}\big(b^{n}_{\mu_{Z^{n}_{t}}}\rho^{n}\chi_{2R}\big)\chi_{R}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{-3}_{1;a})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|a^{n}_{\mu_{Z^{n}_{t}}}\rho^{n}\chi_{2R}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{1}_{z})}+\|\rho^{n}\chi_{2R}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{1}_{z})}+\|b^{n}_{\mu_{Z^{n}_{t}}}\rho^{n}\chi_{2R}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{1}_{z})},

(6.21)

where the implicit constant may depend on
R
, but is independent of
n
.
For the last term, by Hölder’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\|b^{n}_{\mu_{Z^{n}_{t}}}\rho^{n}\chi_{2R}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{1}_{z})}\leqslant\|b^{n}_{\mu_{Z^{n}_{t}}}{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{4R}}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{1}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}}_{z})}\|\rho^{n}{\mathbf{1}}_{Q_{4R}}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\bar{q}_{1}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{\bar{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}}_{z})},

(6.22)

where
\frac{1}{\bar{q}_{1}}+\frac{1}{q_{1}}=\frac{1}{2}
and
\frac{1}{\bar{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}}+\frac{1}{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}}={\mathbf{1}}
.
Since
\frac{2}{q_{1}}+{\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot\frac{1}{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}}<1
, we have
\frac{2}{\bar{q}_{1}}+{\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot(\frac{1}{\bar{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}}-{\mathbf{1}})>0
.
Thus by (
6.16
), (
6.7
), (
6.21
) and (
6.22
), we obtain

\sup_{n}\|\partial_{t}(\rho^{n}\chi_{R})\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{-3}_{1;a})}<\infty.

On the other hand, by (
6.16
) we also have for some
\alpha>0
,

\sup_{n}\|\rho^{n}\chi_{R}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{t}({\mathbf{B}}^{\alpha}_{1;a})}<\infty.

Since
{\mathbf{B}}^{\alpha}_{1;a}
is locally and compactly embedded in
{\mathbb{L}}^{1}_{z}
, by Aubin-Lions’ lemma (see
[
29
]
) and a further diagolization method, there is a subsubsequence
n_{k}
of
n_{m}

so that
{\mathbb{P}}_{n_{k}}
weakly converges to
{\mathbb{P}}
as
k\to\infty
and (
6.20
) holds. The proof is complete.
∎

Convention:
For simplicity of notations, we shall assume that
({\mathbb{P}}_{n})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
weakly converges to
{\mathbb{P}}
and (
6.20
) holds for the whole sequence below.

We need the following technical lemma for taking limits below.

Lemma 6.10
.

Fix
N\in{\mathbb{N}}
. Let
f\in L^{1}_{loc}({\mathbb{R}}^{N+1})
and
\rho_{n},\rho\in L^{1}_{loc}({\mathbb{R}}^{N})
. Suppose that for any
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
and bounded
Q\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
,

\lim_{h\to 0}\int_{Q}\sup_{|r|,|r^{\prime}|\leqslant m,|r-r^{\prime}|\leqslant h}|f(z,r)-f(z,r^{\prime})|{\mathord{{\rm d}}}z=0,\ \ \lim_{n\to\infty}\|\rho_{n}-\rho\|_{L^{1}(Q)}=0.

Let
f_{n}(z,r):=(f*\varGamma_{n})(z,r)
. Then for any
\varepsilon>0
and bounded
Q\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
,

\lim_{n\to\infty}{\mathcal{L}}\Big\{z\in Q:|f_{n}(z,\rho_{n}(z))-f(z,\rho(z))|\geqslant\varepsilon\Big\}=0,

where
{\mathcal{L}}
stands for the Lebesgue measure in
{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
.

Proof.

Fix bounded
Q\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
. For any
m,n\in{\mathbb{N}}
and
h>0
, we define

A^{m}_{n}:=\big\{z\in Q:|\rho_{n}(z)|\vee|\rho(z)|\leqslant m\big\},\ \ D^{h}_{n}:=\big\{z\in Q:|\rho_{n}(z)-\rho(z)|\leqslant h\big\},

and for
\varepsilon>0
,

Q^{\varepsilon}_{n}:=\big\{z\in Q:|f_{n}(z,\rho_{n}(z))-f(z,\rho(z))|\geqslant\varepsilon\big\}.

By the assumption and Chebyschev’s inequality, we clearly have

\displaystyle\lim_{m\to\infty}\sup_{n}{\mathcal{L}}((A^{m}_{n})^{c})=0,\ \ \lim_{n\to\infty}{\mathcal{L}}((D^{h}_{n})^{c})=0,\ \ \forall h>0.

(6.23)

Note that

\displaystyle Q^{\varepsilon}_{n}

\displaystyle\subset\big\{z\in Q:|f(z,\rho_{n}(z))-f(z,\rho(z))|\geqslant\varepsilon/2\big\}

\displaystyle\quad\cup\big\{z\in Q:|f_{n}(z,\rho_{n}(z))-f(z,\rho_{n}(z))|\geqslant\varepsilon/2\big\}=:Q^{\varepsilon,1}_{n}\cup Q^{\varepsilon,2}_{n}.

Thus, to show
\lim_{n\to\infty}{\mathcal{L}}(Q^{\varepsilon}_{n})=0
, by (
6.23
), it suffices to show that for each
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
,

\displaystyle\lim_{h\to 0}\sup_{n}{\mathcal{L}}(Q^{\varepsilon,1}_{n}\cap A^{m}_{n}\cap D^{h}_{n})=0,

(6.24)

and

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}{\mathcal{L}}(Q^{\varepsilon,2}_{n}\cap A^{m}_{n})=0.

(6.25)

For limit (
6.24
), it follows by the following observation and the assumption

Q^{\varepsilon,1}_{n}\cap A^{m}_{n}\cap D^{h}_{n}\subset\Big\{z\in Q:\sup_{|r|,|r^{\prime}|\leqslant m,|r-r^{\prime}|\leqslant h}|f(z,r)-f(z,r^{\prime})|\geqslant\varepsilon/2\Big\}.

For limit (
6.25
), note that by the definition of
f_{n}
and the assumption,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\lim_{h\to 0}\sup_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}\int_{Q}\sup_{|r|\leqslant m}|f_{n}(z,r+h)-f_{n}(z,r)|{\mathord{{\rm d}}}z\\
&\quad\leqslant\lim_{h\to 0}\int_{Q^{\prime}}\sup_{|r|\leqslant m+1}|f(z,r+h)-f(z,r)|{\mathord{{\rm d}}}z=0,\end{split}

(6.26)

where
Q\Subset Q^{\prime}
.
Since for each
r
,

\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{Q}|f_{n}(z,r)-f(z,r)|{\mathord{{\rm d}}}z=0,

by (
6.26
) and a finitely covering technique, we have for each
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
,

\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{Q}\sup_{|r|\leqslant m}|f_{n}(z,r)-f(z,r)|{\mathord{{\rm d}}}z=0,

which in turn implies (
6.25
) by noting that

Q^{\varepsilon,2}_{n}\cap A^{m}_{n}\subset\Big\{z\in Q:\sup_{|r|\leqslant m}|f_{n}(z,r)-f(z,r)|\geqslant\varepsilon/2\Big\}.

The proof is complete.
∎

Now define

\displaystyle\bar{a}_{n}(t,x):=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}a_{n}\Big(t,x,(\langle\rho^{n}_{t}\rangle*\varGamma_{n})(x),z^{\prime}\Big)\rho^{n}_{t}(z^{\prime}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}z^{\prime},

(6.27)

where
\langle\rho^{n}_{t}\rangle(x):=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rho^{n}_{t}(x,v){\mathord{{\rm d}}}v
and

\displaystyle\bar{b}_{n}(t,z):=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}b_{n}\Big(t,z,(\rho^{n}_{t}*\varGamma_{n})(z),z^{\prime}\Big)\rho^{n}_{t}(z^{\prime}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}z^{\prime}.

(6.28)

We have

Lemma 6.11
.

For any bounded domain
Q\subset{\mathbb{R}}_{+}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}
, it holds that for any
(q_{0},{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0})\in[1,\infty)^{1+2d}
,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\|(\bar{a}_{n}-a_{\rho}){\mathbf{1}}_{Q}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{0}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}}_{z})}=0,

(6.29)

and for any
q_{0}<q_{1}
and
{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}<{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}
, where
(q_{1},{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1})
is from (
1.9
),

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\|(\bar{b}_{n}-b_{\rho}){\mathbf{1}}_{Q}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{0}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}}_{z})}=0,

(6.30)

where
a_{\rho}
and
b_{\rho}
are defined by (
6.3
) and (
6.4
) in terms of
\rho
in Lemma
6.9
.

Proof.

We only prove (
6.30
) since (
6.29
) is completely the same. Note that by (
1.9
) and (
5.5
),

\sup_{n}\|(\bar{b}_{n}-b_{\rho}){\mathbf{1}}_{Q}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{1}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}}_{z})}\lesssim|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|h|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{1}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}}_{z})}.

It suffices to prove that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\|(\bar{b}_{n}-b_{\rho}){\mathbf{1}}_{Q}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{1}}=0.

For
R\geqslant 1
, let
B_{R}:=\{z\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}:|z|\leqslant R\}
.
By (
6.28
) and (
6.4
), we make the following decomposition:

\displaystyle(\bar{b}_{n}-b_{\rho})(t,z)

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}b_{n}\big(t,z,(\rho^{n}_{t}*\varGamma_{n})(z),z^{\prime}\big)\rho_{t}^{n}(z^{\prime}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}z^{\prime}-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}b\big(t,z,\rho_{t}(z),z\big)\rho_{t}(z^{\prime}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}z^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\left\{\int_{B^{c}_{R}}b_{n}\big(t,z,(\rho^{n}_{t}*\varGamma_{n})(z),z^{\prime}\big)\rho_{t}^{n}(z^{\prime}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}z^{\prime}-\int_{B^{c}_{R}}b\big(t,z,\rho_{t}(z),z^{\prime}\big)\rho_{t}(z^{\prime}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}z^{\prime}\right\}

\displaystyle+\left\{\int_{B_{R}}b_{n}\big(t,z,(\rho^{n}_{t}*\varGamma_{n})(z),z^{\prime}\big)\rho_{t}^{n}(z^{\prime}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}z^{\prime}-\int_{B_{R}}b\big(t,z,\rho_{t}(z),z^{\prime}\big)\rho_{t}(z^{\prime}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}z^{\prime}\right\}

\displaystyle=:I^{n}_{R}(t,z)+J^{n}_{R}(t,z).

For
I^{n}_{R}
, noting that by (
1.9
) and Minkowskii’s inequality,

\displaystyle|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|I^{n}_{R}|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{1}}\leqslant|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|h|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{1}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left(\int_{B^{c}_{R}}\rho_{t}^{n}(z^{\prime}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}z^{\prime}+\int_{B^{c}_{R}}\rho_{t}(z^{\prime}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}z^{\prime}\right),

by the tightness of
({\mathbb{P}}_{n})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
, we get

\lim_{R\to\infty}\sup_{n}|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|I^{n}_{R}|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{1}}=0.

Thus it remains to show that for each
R\geqslant 1
,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\|J^{n}_{R}{\mathbf{1}}_{Q}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{1}}=0.

(6.31)

We make the following decomposition for
J^{n}_{R}
,

\displaystyle J^{n}_{R}(t,z)

\displaystyle=\int_{B_{R}}\Big(b_{n}\big(t,z,(\rho^{n}_{t}*\varGamma_{n})(z),z^{\prime}\big)-b\big(t,z,\rho_{t}(z),z^{\prime}\big)\Big)\rho_{t}(z^{\prime}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}z^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\int_{B_{R}}b_{n}\big(t,z,(\rho^{n}_{t}*\varGamma_{n})(z),z^{\prime}\big)\Big(\rho_{t}^{n}(z^{\prime})-\rho_{t}(z^{\prime})\Big){\mathord{{\rm d}}}z^{\prime}=:J^{1,n}_{R}(t,z)+J^{2,n}_{R}(t,z).

For
J^{1,n}_{R}
, noting that by (
6.20
),

\lim_{n\to\infty}\|(\rho^{n}*\varGamma_{n}-\rho){\mathbf{1}}_{Q}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{1}}=0,

by (
1.8
) and Lemma
6.10
, we have for each
\varepsilon>0
,

\lim_{n\to\infty}{\mathcal{L}}\Big\{(t,z,z^{\prime})\in Q:|b_{n}\big(t,z,(\rho^{n}_{t}*\varGamma_{n})(z),z^{\prime}\big)-b\big(t,z,\rho_{t}(z),z^{\prime}\big)|\geqslant\varepsilon\Big\}=0.

Thus, by the uniform integrability of
J^{1,n}_{R}{\mathbf{1}}_{Q}
,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\|J^{1,n}_{R}{\mathbf{1}}_{Q}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{1}}=0.

(6.32)

For
J^{2,n}_{R}
, by (
1.9
), (
6.20
) and Hölder’s inequality, we have

\lim_{n\to\infty}|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|J^{2,n}_{R}|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{1}}\leqslant|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|h|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{t}(\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{1}_{z})}\lim_{n\to\infty}\|(\rho^{n}-\rho){\mathbf{1}}_{B_{R}}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{1}_{z})}=0,

which together with (
6.32
) yields (
6.31
). The proof is complete.
∎

Now we can give the proof of Theorem
6.3
.

Proof of Theorem
6.3
.

Let
{\mathbb{P}}
be the accumulation point in Lemma
6.9
. We aim to show
{\mathbb{P}}\in{\mathscr{M}}^{a,b}_{\nu}
.
More precisely, we need to show that for each
t_{1}>t_{0}\geqslant 0
and bounded continuous
{\mathcal{B}}_{t_{0}}
-measurable functional
G_{t_{0}}
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbb{P}}}(M_{t_{1}}G_{t_{0}})={\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbb{P}}}(M_{t_{0}}G_{t_{0}}),

(6.33)

where
M_{t}
is defined by (
6.2
).
Below we simply write
{\mathbb{E}}:={\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbb{P}}}
and
{\mathbb{E}}_{n}:={\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbb{P}}_{n}}
.
By SDE (
6.11
) and Itô’s formula, it is easy to see that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{n}(M^{n}_{t_{1}}G_{t_{0}})={\mathbf{E}}(M^{n}_{t_{1}}(Z^{n}_{\cdot})G_{t_{0}}(Z^{n}_{\cdot}))={\mathbf{E}}(M^{n}_{t_{0}}(Z^{n}_{\cdot})G_{t_{0}}(Z^{n}_{\cdot}))={\mathbb{E}}_{n}(M^{n}_{t_{0}}G_{t_{0}}),

(6.34)

where

M^{n}_{t}(\omega):=f(\omega_{t})-f(\omega_{0})-\int^{t}_{0}\left(\mathrm{tr}(\bar{a}_{n}\cdot\nabla^{2}_{v}f)+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f+\bar{b}_{n}\cdot\nabla_{v}f\right)(r,\omega_{r}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}r,

where
\bar{a}_{n}
and
\bar{b}_{n}
are defined by (
6.27
) and (
6.28
).
Note that (
6.34
) is equivalent to

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{n}\left(G_{t_{0}}\int^{t_{1}}_{t_{0}}\left(\mathrm{tr}(\bar{a}_{n}\cdot\nabla^{2}_{v}f)+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f+\bar{b}_{n}\cdot\nabla_{v}f\right)(r,\omega_{r}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}r\right)=0.

(6.35)

We use Lemma
6.5
and Lemma
6.11
to show the following limits

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}{\mathbb{E}}_{n}\left(G_{t_{0}}\int^{t_{1}}_{t_{0}}\mathrm{tr}(\bar{a}_{n}\cdot\nabla^{2}_{v}f)(r,\omega_{r}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}r\right)={\mathbb{E}}\left(G_{t_{0}}\int^{t_{1}}_{t_{0}}\mathrm{tr}(a_{\rho}\cdot\nabla^{2}_{v}f)(r,\omega_{r}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}r\right)

(6.36)

and

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}{\mathbb{E}}_{n}\left(G_{t_{0}}\int^{t_{1}}_{t_{0}}(\bar{b}_{n}\cdot\nabla_{v}f)(r,\omega_{r}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}r\right)={\mathbb{E}}\left(G_{t_{0}}\int^{t_{1}}_{t_{0}}(b_{\rho}\cdot\nabla_{v}f)(r,\omega_{r}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}r\right).

(6.37)

We only show (
6.37
) since (
6.36
) is completely the same. For (
6.37
), it follows from the following two limits:

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}{\mathbb{E}}_{n}\left(G_{t_{0}}\int^{t_{1}}_{t_{0}}((\bar{b}_{n}-b_{\rho})\cdot\nabla_{v}f)(r,\omega_{r}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}r\right)=0,

(6.38)

and

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}{\mathbb{E}}_{n}\left(G_{t_{0}}\int^{t_{1}}_{t_{0}}(b_{\rho}\cdot\nabla_{v}f)(r,\omega_{r}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}r\right)={\mathbb{E}}\left(G_{t_{0}}\int^{t_{1}}_{t_{0}}(b_{\rho}\cdot\nabla_{v}f)(r,\omega_{r}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}r\right).

(6.39)

We first show (
6.38
). Let

q_{0}:=q_{1}/2,\ \ {\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}:={\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}/2.

For any
R\geqslant 1
, since
\frac{2}{q_{0}}+{\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot\frac{1}{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}}<2
,
by Krylov’s estimate (
6.12
) and (
6.30
), we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{n}\left(\int^{t_{1}}_{t_{0}}\big|{\mathbf{1}}_{B_{R}}(\bar{b}_{n}-b_{\rho})\big|(r,\omega_{r}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}r\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\|{\mathbf{1}}_{[t_{0},t_{1}]\times B_{R}}(\bar{b}_{n}-b_{\rho})\big\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{0}}_{t}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}}_{z})}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle n\to\infty}}{{\to}}0,

and by Hölder’s inequality,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{n}\left(\int^{t_{1}}_{t_{0}}\big|{\mathbf{1}}_{B^{c}_{R}}(\bar{b}_{n}-b_{\rho})\big|(r,\omega_{r}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}r\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\int^{t_{1}}_{t_{0}}{\mathbb{P}}_{n}(|\omega_{r}|\geqslant R){\mathord{{\rm d}}}r\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left({\mathbb{E}}_{n}\int^{t_{1}}_{t_{0}}\big|\bar{b}_{n}-b_{\rho}\big|^{2}(r,\omega_{r}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}r\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{r\in[t_{0},t_{1}]}({\mathbb{P}}_{n}(|\omega_{r}|\geqslant R))^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}||\bar{b}_{n}-b_{\rho}|^{2}|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{0}}_{t}(\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}}_{z})}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\sup_{r\in[t_{0},t_{1}]}({\mathbb{P}}_{n}(|\omega_{r}|\geqslant R))^{\frac{1}{2}}|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|\bar{b}_{n}-b_{\rho}|\mspace{-3mu}|\mspace{-3mu}|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{1}}_{t}(\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}}_{z})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{r\in[t_{0},t_{1}]}({\mathbf{P}}(|Z^{n}_{r}|\geqslant R))^{\frac{1}{2}},

which converges to zero uniformly in
n
as
R\to\infty
by the proof of Lemma
6.8
.
Thus we obtain (
6.38
). Next we look at (
6.39
). Let

b^{m}_{\rho}(t,z):=b_{\rho}(t,\cdot)*\varGamma_{m}(z).

As above, by Krylov’s estimate (
6.12
), we have

\displaystyle\lim_{m\to\infty}\sup_{n}{\mathbb{E}}_{n}\left(\int^{t_{1}}_{t_{0}}|(b^{m}_{\rho}-b_{\rho})\cdot\nabla_{v}f|(r,\omega_{r}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}r\right)=0.

(6.40)

Moreover, for fixed
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
, since
\omega\to G_{t_{0}}(\omega)\int^{t_{1}}_{t_{0}}(b^{m}_{\rho}\cdot\nabla_{v}f)(r,\omega_{r}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}r

is a bounded continuous functional, we have

\lim_{n\to\infty}{\mathbb{E}}_{n}\left(G_{t_{0}}\int^{t_{1}}_{t_{0}}(b^{m}_{\rho}\cdot\nabla_{v}f)(r,\omega_{r}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}r\right)={\mathbb{E}}\left(G_{t_{0}}\int^{t_{1}}_{t_{0}}(b^{m}_{\rho}\cdot\nabla_{v}f)(r,\omega_{r}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}r\right),

which together with (
6.40
) yields (
6.39
).
Thus, by taking limits
n\to\infty
for (
6.35
), we obtain

{\mathbb{E}}\left(G_{t_{0}}\int^{t_{1}}_{t_{0}}\left(\mathrm{tr}(a_{\rho}\cdot\nabla^{2}_{v}f)+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f+b_{\rho}\cdot\nabla_{v}f\right)(r,\omega_{r}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}r\right)=0,

which implies (
6.33
). As for the regularity (
1.11
), it follows by (
6.16
) and (
6.20
). The proof is complete.
∎

Remark 6.12
.

Suppose that the coefficients do not depend on the density, that is,
b(t,z,r,z^{\prime})
and
a(t,x,r,z^{\prime})
are independent of
r
,
then we can drop the assumption
q_{1}\in(2,4)
in
(H
1
)
but require
\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}}+\frac{{\mathbf{1}}}{q_{1}}<\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{2}}}
(see Remark
4.4
).
In this case, we can use the Kyrlov estimate (
6.15
) and follow the same argument as in
[
27
]
to take the limits.

7.
Well-posedness of generalized martingale problems

In this section we show the well-posedness for a class of generalized martingale problems of SDE (
6.1
)
when diffusion matrix does not depend on the distribution, but may be discontinuous in position variable
x
.
Instead of
(H
1
)
and
(H
2
)
, we suppose that
(H
3
)
holds.
Fix
T>0
and
f\in C^{\infty}_{c}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})
. Consider the following backward Kolmogorov equation:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}u+\mathrm{tr}(a\cdot\nabla^{2}_{v}u)+v\cdot\nabla_{x}u+b_{\rho}\cdot\nabla_{v}u=f,\ \ u(T)=0,

(7.1)

where for a family of density functions
\rho_{t}(z)
in
{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}
,

b_{\rho}(t,z):=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}b(t,z,\rho_{t}(z),z^{\prime})\rho_{t}(z^{\prime}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}z^{\prime}.

Since
b_{\rho}
is bounded measurable and
a
does not depend on
v
, by reversing the time variable and Theorem
5.2
,
there is a unique weak solution
u^{f}_{\rho}
to (
7.1
) with

u^{f}_{\rho}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{V}}_{T}\cap C_{b}([0,T]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}).

Here the continuity of
u^{f}_{\rho}
is crux for the following notion of generalized martingale solutions.

Definition 7.1

(Generalized martingale problem)
.

Let
s\geqslant 0
and
\nu\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})
. A probability measure
{\mathbb{P}}\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{C}})
is called a generalized martingale solution of DDSDE (
6.1
) starting from
\nu

at time
s
if

(i)

{\mathbb{P}}\circ\omega^{-1}_{s}=\nu
, and for Lebesgue almost all
t\geqslant s
,
{\mathbb{P}}\circ\omega_{t}^{-1}({\mathord{{\rm d}}}z)=\rho_{t}(z){\mathord{{\rm d}}}z
.

(ii)

For any
T>s
and
f\in C^{\infty}_{c}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})
, the process

\displaystyle M_{t}:=u^{f}_{\rho}(t,\omega_{t})-u^{f}_{\rho}(s,\omega_{s})-\int^{t}_{s}f(\omega_{r}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}r,\ \ t\in[s,T],

(7.2)

is a
{\mathcal{B}}_{t}
-martingale with respect to
{\mathbb{P}}
, where
u^{f}_{\rho}
is the unique solution of (
7.1
).

The set of all the generalized martingale solutions
{\mathbb{P}}\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{C}})
with initial distribution
\nu
at time
s
is denoted by
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{a,b}_{s,\nu}
.

Remark 7.2
.

The above notion of martingale solutions was introduced in its most general form in
[
9
, Chapter 4]
.
It should be noticed that if (
7.1
) has a
C^{2}
-solution
u
, then by Itô’s formula, any weak solution of (
6.1
) must be a generalized martingale solution. In general,
these two notions are not equivalent.

Now we can give

Proof of Theorem
1.5
.

(Existence)
Without loss of generality we assume
s=0
.
As in Section
6
, we consider the approximating SDE (
6.11
). Let
{\mathbb{P}}\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{C}})
be an accumulation point of
({\mathbb{P}}_{n})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
.
In particular, by Lemma
6.8
,

{\mathbb{P}}_{n}\circ\omega_{t}^{-1}(z)=\rho^{n}_{t}(z){\mathord{{\rm d}}}z,\ \ {\mathbb{P}}\circ\omega_{t}^{-1}(z)=\rho_{t}(z){\mathord{{\rm d}}}z.

In the following we use the convention before Lemma
6.10
.
Recall
a_{n}(t,x)=a(t,\cdot)*\varGamma_{n}(x)
and
\bar{b}_{n}
being defined by (
6.28
).
Since for each
\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{c}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})
, by Itô’s formula,

\partial_{t}\int\varphi\rho^{n}_{t}=\int\mathrm{tr}(a_{n}\cdot\nabla^{2}_{v}\varphi)\rho^{n}_{t}+\int(v\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi)\rho^{n}_{t}+\int(\bar{b}_{n}\cdot\nabla_{v}\varphi)\rho^{n}_{t},

by Lemmas
6.9
and
6.11
and taking limits, it is easy to see that

\partial_{t}\int\varphi\rho_{t}=\int\mathrm{tr}(a\cdot\nabla^{2}_{v}\varphi)\rho_{t}+\int(v\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi)\rho_{t}+\int(b_{\rho}\cdot\nabla_{v}\varphi)\rho_{t}.

In other words,
\rho_{t}
solves (
1.12
) in the distributional sense.
Fix
T>0
and
f\in C^{\infty}_{c}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})
. Let
u_{n}\in L^{\infty}_{T}(C^{\infty}_{b}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d}))
be the unique smooth solution of the following backward PDE:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}u_{n}+\mathrm{tr}(a_{n}\cdot\nabla^{2}_{v}u_{n})+v\cdot\nabla_{x}u_{n}+\bar{b}_{n}\cdot\nabla_{v}u_{n}=f,\ \ u_{n}(T)=0.

(7.3)

By Lemma
6.11
and Theorem
5.2
, we have
for any bounded
Q\subset(0,T)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}
,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{(t,z)\in Q}|u_{n}(t,z)-u^{f}_{\rho}(t,z)|=0,

(7.4)

where
u^{f}_{\rho}
is the unique weak solution of PDE (
7.1
).
Now we show that for each
T\geqslant t_{1}>t_{0}\geqslant 0
and bounded continuous
{\mathcal{B}}_{t_{0}}
-measurable functional
G_{t_{0}}
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbb{P}}}(M_{t_{1}}G_{t_{0}})={\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbb{P}}}(M_{t_{0}}G_{t_{0}}),

(7.5)

where
M_{t}
is defined by (
7.2
).
By SDE (
6.11
), Itô’s formula and (
7.3
), it is easy to see that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbb{P}}_{n}}(M^{n}_{t_{1}}G_{t_{0}})={\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbb{P}}_{n}}(M^{n}_{t_{0}}G_{t_{0}}),

(7.6)

where

M^{n}_{t}:=u_{n}(t,\omega_{t})-u_{n}(0,\omega_{0})-\int^{t}_{0}f(\omega_{r}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}r,

By taking limits for both sides of (
7.6
) and using the pointwise convergence (
7.4
), we obtain (
7.5
).

(Uniqueness)
We divide the proof into three steps.

(
Step 1
) First of all, we show the uniqueness for linear SDE, i.e.,
b(t,z,r,z^{\prime})=b(t,z)
does not depend on variables
r,z^{\prime}
.
Let
{\mathbb{P}}_{1},{\mathbb{P}}_{2}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{M}}^{a,b}_{s,\nu}
be two solutions of the generalized martingale problem. Fix
T>s
and
f\in C^{\infty}_{c}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})
.
Let
u^{f}
be the unique weak solution of (see Theorem
5.2
),

\partial_{t}u^{f}+\mathrm{tr}(a\cdot\nabla^{2}_{v}u^{f})+v\cdot\nabla_{x}u^{f}+b\cdot\nabla_{v}u^{f}=f,\ \ u^{f}(T)=0.

By Definition
7.1
and
u^{f}(T)=0
, we have

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}u^{f}(s,z)\nu({\mathord{{\rm d}}}z)=-{\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbb{P}}_{i}}\int_{s}^{T}f(\omega_{r}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}r,\quad i=1,2,

which means that for each
T>s
,

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{T}{\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbb{P}}_{1}}f(\omega_{r}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}r=\int_{s}^{T}{\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbb{P}}_{2}}f(\omega_{r}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}r.

Hence, for any
f\in C^{\infty}_{c}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbb{P}}_{1}}f(\omega_{T})={\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbb{P}}_{2}}f(\omega_{T}),\ \ \forall T>s.

(7.7)

From this, by a standard way (see Theorem 4.4.2 in
[
9
]
), we derive that

{\mathbb{P}}_{1}={\mathbb{P}}_{2}.

Indeed, it suffices to prove the following claim by induction:

(C
n
)
for given
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, and for any
s\leqslant t_{1}<t_{2}<t_{n}<T
and strictly positive and bounded measurable functions
g_{1},\cdots,g_{n}
on
{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbb{P}}_{1}}(g_{1}(\omega_{t_{1}})\cdots g_{n}(\omega_{t_{n}}))={\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbb{P}}_{2}}(g_{1}(\omega_{t_{1}})\cdots g_{n}(\omega_{t_{n}})).

(7.8)

By (
7.7
),
(C
1
)
holds.
Suppose now that
(C
n
)
holds for some
n\geqslant 2
. For simplicity we write

\eta:=g_{1}(\omega_{t_{1}})\cdots g_{n}(\omega_{t_{n}})>0,

and for
i=1,2
, we define new probability measures

{\mathord{{\rm d}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{i}:=\eta{\mathord{{\rm d}}}{\mathbb{P}}_{i}/({\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbb{P}}_{i}}\eta)\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{D}}),\ \ \widetilde{\nu}_{i}:=\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{i}\circ\omega^{-1}_{t_{n}}\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d}).

Now we show

\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{i}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{a,b}_{t_{n};\widetilde{\nu}_{i}},\ \ i=1,2.

For any
f\in C^{\infty}_{c}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})
, let

M_{t}:=u^{f}(t,\omega_{t})-u^{f}(t_{n},\omega_{t_{n}})-\int^{t}_{t_{n}}f(\omega_{r}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}r,\ \ t\in[t_{n},T].

We only need to prove that for any
T\geqslant t^{\prime}>t\geqslant t_{n}
and bounded
{\mathcal{B}}_{t}
-measurable
\xi
,

{\mathbb{E}}^{\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{i}}\left(M_{t^{\prime}}\xi\right)={\mathbb{E}}^{\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{i}}\left(M_{t}\xi\right)\Leftrightarrow{\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbb{P}}_{i}}(M_{t^{\prime}}\xi\eta)={\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbb{P}}_{i}}(M_{t}\xi\eta),

which follows from
{\mathbb{P}}_{i}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{a,b}_{s;\nu}
,
i=1,2
. Thus, by induction hypothesis and (
7.7
),

\widetilde{\nu}_{1}=\widetilde{\nu}_{2}\Rightarrow\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{1}\circ\omega^{-1}_{t_{n+1}}=\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{2}\circ\omega^{-1}_{t_{n+1}},\ \ \forall T\geqslant t_{n+1}>t_{n}.

which in turn implies that
(C
n+1
)
holds.

(
Step 2
) For general nonlinear SDE, we use Girsanov’s trasformation method
(see
[
27
,
13
]
). Without loss of generality, we assume
s=0
. Let
{\mathbb{P}}_{1},{\mathbb{P}}_{2}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{M}}^{a,b}_{0,\nu}
be two solutions of the generalized martingale problem.
Let

a_{n}:=a*\varGamma_{n},\ \ b_{n}:=b*\varGamma_{n},\ \ \rho^{n}_{0}:=\rho_{0}*\varGamma_{n},

and for
{\mathbb{P}}_{i}\circ\omega_{t}^{-1}({\mathord{{\rm d}}}z)=\rho^{i}_{t}(z){\mathord{{\rm d}}}z
,

\displaystyle\bar{b}^{(i)}_{n}(t,z):=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}b_{n}(t,z,(\rho^{i}_{t}*\varGamma_{n})(z),z^{\prime})\rho^{i}_{t}(z^{\prime}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}z^{\prime}.

(7.9)

Note that for any
T>0
,

a_{n},\bar{b}^{(i)}_{n}\in L^{\infty}_{T}(C^{\infty}_{b}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})),\ \ \|a_{n}\|_{\infty}\leqslant\|a\|_{\infty},\ \ \|\bar{b}^{(i)}_{n}\|_{\infty}\leqslant\|b\|_{\infty}.

We consider the following approximation of linearized SDEs: for
i=1,2
,

\displaystyle{\mathord{{\rm d}}}X^{i,n}_{t}=V^{i,n}_{t}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}t,\ {\mathord{{\rm d}}}V^{i,n}_{t}=\bar{b}^{(i)}_{n}(t,Z^{i,n}_{t}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}t+\sqrt{2a_{n}}(t,X^{i,n}_{t}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}W_{t},

(7.10)

where
Z^{i,n}_{0}
is an
{\mathscr{F}}_{0}
-measurable random variable and has smooth density
\rho^{n}_{0}
.
Since by Lemma
6.10
,

\bar{b}^{(i)}_{n}(t,z)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle n\to\infty}}{{\to}}b_{\rho^{i}}(t,z)=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}b(t,z,\rho^{i}_{t}(z),z^{\prime})\rho^{i}_{t}(z^{\prime}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}z^{\prime}\mbox{ in Lebesgue measure ${\mathord{{\rm d}}}t{\mathord{{\rm d}}}z$,}

as in the proof of the existence part,
and due to the uniqueness of linear equations, for
i=1,2
,
the law of
Z^{i,n}
weakly converges to
{\mathbb{P}}_{i}
as
n\to\infty
. In particular, for any
\varphi\in C_{b}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})
,

\displaystyle{\mathbf{E}}\varphi(Z^{i,n}_{t})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle n\to\infty}}{{\to}}{\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbb{P}}_{i}}\varphi(\omega_{t}),\ \ i=1,2.

(7.11)

Since
a_{n},\bar{b}^{(i)}_{n}\in L^{\infty}_{T}(C^{\infty}_{b}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d}))
and
\rho^{n}_{0}\in C^{\infty}_{b}
,
it is well known that the density
\rho^{i,n}_{t}
of
Z^{i,n}_{t}
is smooth and uniquely solves the following FPKE:

\partial_{t}\rho^{i,n}_{t}=\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}(a_{n}\cdot\nabla_{v}\rho^{i,n}_{t})-v\cdot\nabla_{x}\rho^{i,n}_{t}+\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}(\bar{b}^{(i)}_{n}\rho^{i,n}_{t}),

where we have used that
a_{n}
does not depend on the velocity variable
v
. By Theorem
5.3
with
b_{1}=0
,

b_{2}=\bar{b}^{(i)}_{n}
and
g=0
, for any
T>0
, there is a constant
C>0
such that for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{1}}_{[0,T]}\rho^{i,n}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}}\lesssim_{C}\|\rho^{n}_{0}\|_{C^{1}_{b}}\lesssim_{C}\|\rho_{0}\|_{C^{1}_{b}},\ \ i=1,2.

(7.12)

(
Step 3
) To perform the Girsanov transformation, for
i=1,2
and
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, we define

\displaystyle H^{(i)}_{n}(s,z):=(\sqrt{2a_{n}})^{-1}(s,x)\cdot\bar{b}^{(i)}_{n}(s,z),\ \ z=(x,v),

(7.13)

and

A^{i,n}_{t}:=\exp\left\{-\int^{t}_{0}H^{(i)}_{n}(s,Z^{i,n}_{s}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}W_{s}-\frac{1}{2}\int^{t}_{0}|H^{(i)}_{n}(s,Z^{i,n}_{s})|^{2}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}s\right\}.

Fix
T>0
. Since for some
C>0
independent of
n
,

\displaystyle\|H^{(i)}_{n}\|_{\infty}\leqslant C,\ \ i=1,2,

(7.14)

by Girsanov’s theorem, for
i=1,2
, under the new probability measure
Q^{i,n}:=A^{i,n}_{T}{\mathbf{P}}
,

\widetilde{W}^{i,n}_{t}:=\int^{t}_{0}H^{(i)}_{n}(s,Z^{i,n}_{s}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}s+W_{t},\ t\in[0,T],

is still a Brownian motion, and

{\mathord{{\rm d}}}X^{i,n}_{t}=V^{i,n}_{t}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}t,\ {\mathord{{\rm d}}}V^{i,n}_{t}=\sqrt{2a_{n}}(t,X^{i,n}_{t}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}\widetilde{W}^{i,n}_{t}.

Since
\sqrt{a_{n}}
is Lipschitz, the above SDE admits a unique weak solution. Thus

Q^{1,n}\circ(Z^{1,n})^{-1}=Q^{2,n}\circ(Z^{2,n})^{-1},

equivalently, for any nonnegative functional
G
on
{\mathbb{C}}_{T}:=C([0,T];{\mathbb{R}}^{2d})
,

{\mathbf{E}}(G(Z^{1,n}_{\cdot})A^{1,n}_{T})={\mathbf{E}}(G(Z^{2,n}_{\cdot})A^{2,n}_{T}).

In particular, for any
\varphi\in C_{b}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})
,

\displaystyle{\mathbf{E}}\varphi(Z^{1,n}_{T})={\mathbf{E}}(\varphi(Z^{2,n}_{T})Y^{n}_{T}),

where

Y^{n}_{T}:=\exp\left\{\int^{T}_{0}(H^{(1)}_{n}-H^{(2)}_{n})(s,Z^{2,n}_{s}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}W_{s}+\frac{1}{2}\int^{T}_{0}(|H^{(1)}_{n}|^{2}-|H^{(2)}_{n}|^{2})(s,Z^{2,n}_{s}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}s\right\}.

Thus, by Hölder’s inequality,

\displaystyle|{\mathbf{E}}\varphi(Z^{1,n}_{T})-{\mathbf{E}}\varphi(Z^{2,n}_{T})|

\displaystyle=|{\mathbf{E}}(\varphi(Z^{2,n}_{T})(Y^{n}_{T}-1))|\leqslant\|\varphi(Z^{2,n}_{T})\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|Y^{n}_{T}-1\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big(\|\varphi\|_{\infty}\wedge\|\varphi\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{z}}\Big)\|Y^{n}_{T}-1\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)},

(7.15)

where the implicit constant is independent of
n
, and we have used (
7.12
) to derive that

\|\varphi(Z^{2,n}_{T})\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}=\left(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}|\varphi(z)|^{2}\rho^{2,n}_{T}(z){\mathord{{\rm d}}}z\right)^{1/2}\lesssim\|\varphi\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{z}}.

On the other hand, by Itô’s formula, we have

\displaystyle Y^{n}_{T}-1

\displaystyle=\int^{T}_{0}Y^{n}_{s}(H^{(1)}_{n}-H^{(2)}_{n})(s,Z^{2,n}_{s}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}W_{s}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int^{T}_{0}Y^{n}_{s}(|H^{(1)}_{n}|^{2}-|H^{(2)}_{n}|^{2})(s,Z^{2,n}_{s}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}s

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int^{T}_{0}Y^{n}_{s}|H^{(1)}_{n}-H^{(2)}_{n}|^{2}(s,Z^{2,n}_{s}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}s.

By BDG’s inequality and (
7.14
), we have

\displaystyle{\mathbf{E}}|Y^{n}_{T}-1|^{2}\lesssim\int^{T}_{0}{\mathbf{E}}|Y^{n}_{s}-1|^{2}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}s+\int^{T}_{0}{\mathbf{E}}\big(|H^{(1)}_{n}-H^{(2)}_{n}|^{2}(s,Z^{2,n}_{s})\big){\mathord{{\rm d}}}s,

which implies by Gronwall’s inequality that

{\mathbf{E}}|Y^{n}_{T}-1|^{2}\lesssim\int^{T}_{0}{\mathbf{E}}\big(|H^{(1)}_{n}-H^{(2)}_{n}|^{2}(s,Z^{2,n}_{s})\big){\mathord{{\rm d}}}s.

Note that by (
7.9
), (
7.13
) and
(H
3
)
,

\displaystyle|H^{(1)}_{n}-H^{(2)}_{n}|(t,z)\leqslant\|(\sqrt{2a_{n}})^{-1}\|_{\infty}|\bar{b}^{(1)}_{n}-\bar{b}^{(2)}_{n}|(t,z)\lesssim|(\rho^{1}_{t}-\rho^{2}_{t})*\varGamma_{n}|(z)+\|\rho^{1}_{t}-\rho^{2}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{1}_{z}}.

Therefore, by (
7.12
) again,

\displaystyle{\mathbf{E}}|Y^{n}_{T}-1|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int^{T}_{0}{\mathbf{E}}|(\rho^{1}_{s}-\rho^{2}_{s})*\varGamma_{n}|^{2}(Z^{2,n}_{s}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}s+\int^{T}_{0}\|\rho_{s}^{1}-\rho_{s}^{2}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{1}_{z}}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}s

\displaystyle\lesssim\int^{T}_{0}\|(\rho^{1}_{s}-\rho^{2}_{s})*\varGamma_{n}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{z}}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}s+\int^{T}_{0}\|\rho_{s}^{1}-\rho_{s}^{2}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{1}_{z}}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}s

\displaystyle\lesssim\int^{T}_{0}\|\rho^{1}_{s}-\rho^{2}_{s}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{z}}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}s+\int^{T}_{0}\|\rho_{s}^{1}-\rho_{s}^{2}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{1}_{z}}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}s

\displaystyle\lesssim\int^{T}_{0}\Big(\|\rho^{1}_{s}-\rho^{2}_{s}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{z}}\vee\|\rho_{s}^{1}-\rho_{s}^{2}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{1}_{z}}\Big){\mathord{{\rm d}}}s.

(7.16)

Combining (
7.15
) and (
7.16
) and by (
7.11
), we obtain that for all
\varphi\in C_{b}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})
,

\displaystyle|{\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbb{P}}_{1}}\varphi(\omega_{T})-{\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbb{P}}_{2}}\varphi(\omega_{T})|=\lim_{n\to\infty}|{\mathbf{E}}\varphi(Z^{1,n}_{T})-{\mathbf{E}}\varphi(Z^{2,n}_{T})|

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\Big(\|\varphi\|_{\infty}\wedge\|\varphi\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{z}}\Big)\left(\int^{T}_{0}\Big(\|\rho_{s}^{1}-\rho_{s}^{2}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{z}}\vee\|\rho^{1}_{s}-\rho^{2}_{s}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{1}_{z}}\Big){\mathord{{\rm d}}}s\right)^{1/2}.

Since for Lebesgue almost all
T>0
,
{\mathbb{P}}_{i}\circ\omega_{T}^{-1}({\mathord{{\rm d}}}z)=\rho_{T}^{i}(z){\mathord{{\rm d}}}z
, the above inequality means that for Lebesgue almost all
T>0
,

\displaystyle\|\rho_{T}^{1}-\rho_{T}^{2}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{z}}\vee\|\rho^{1}_{T}-\rho^{2}_{T}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{1}_{z}}\lesssim\left(\int^{T}_{0}\Big(\|\rho_{s}^{1}-\rho_{s}^{2}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{z}}\vee\|\rho^{1}_{s}-\rho^{2}_{s}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{1}_{z}}\Big){\mathord{{\rm d}}}s\right)^{1/2},

(7.17)

which in turn implies that
\rho^{1}_{T}=\rho^{2}_{T}
by Gronwall’s inequality. Finally, as usual we use the uniqueness for linear equations in
Step 1
to derive
{\mathbb{P}}_{1}={\mathbb{P}}_{2}
. The proof is complete.
∎

Remark 7.3
.

(i) From the proof of the existence part, it is easy to see that the unique generalized martingale solution
{\mathbb{P}}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{M}}^{a,b}_{0,\nu}
also belongs to
{\mathscr{M}}^{a,b}_{\nu}
, i.e., it is also a classical martingale solution.

(ii) If
b
does not depend on the density variable
r
, then we can drop the assumption on the initial distribution
\nu
since in this case the boundedness (
7.12
) is not needed in the proof and only the total variational norm
\|\rho^{1}_{s}-\rho^{2}_{s}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{1}_{z}}
is used in (
7.17
).
